inside the first expectation:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\bar{q}(Z,a,X)\{h(W,a,X)-\bar{h}(W,a,X)\}\mid A=a\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[(\bar{q}(Z,a,X)-q(Z,a,X))(h(W,a,X)-\bar{h}(W,a,X))\mid A=a\right]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\left[q(Z,a,X)(h(W,a,X)-\bar{h}(W,a,X))\mid A=a\right].

Hence

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\xi_{\mathrm{prox}}(O;\bar{q},\bar{h})\mid A=a\right]-\theta(a)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[(\bar{q}(Z,a,X)-q(Z,a,X))(h(W,a,X)-\bar{h}(W,a,X))\mid A=a\right]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\left[q(Z,a,X)(h(W,a,X)-\bar{h}(W,a,X))\mid A=a\right]-\{\mathbb{E}[h(W,a,X)]-\mathbb{E}[\bar{h}(W,a,X)]\}.

It remains to simplify the last two terms. To this end, apply Lemma
1
with

g(W,X)=h(W,a,X)-\bar{h}(W,a,X).

Lemma
1
yields

\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{f_{A}(a)}{f(a\mid W,X)}\{h(W,a,X)-\bar{h}(W,a,X)\}\mid A=a\right]=\mathbb{E}[h(W,a,X)]-\mathbb{E}[\bar{h}(W,a,X)].

Also, by the treatment bridge equation (
2
) and iterated expectation,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[q(Z,a,X)(h(W,a,X)-\bar{h}(W,a,X))\mid A=a\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[q(Z,a,X)\mid W,X,A=a\right]\{h(W,a,X)-\bar{h}(W,a,X)\}\middle|A=a\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{f_{A}(a)}{f(a\mid W,X)}\{h(W,a,X)-\bar{h}(W,a,X)\}\middle|A=a\right].

Combining the last two displays, we obtain

\mathbb{E}\left[q(Z,a,X)(h(W,a,X)-\bar{h}(W,a,X))\mid A=a\right]=\mathbb{E}[h(W,a,X)]-\mathbb{E}[\bar{h}(W,a,X)].

Therefore these terms cancel, and we conclude that

\mathbb{E}\left[\xi_{\mathrm{prox}}(O;\bar{q},\bar{h})\mid A=a\right]-\theta(a)=\mathbb{E}\left[(\bar{q}(Z,a,X)-q(Z,a,X))(h(W,a,X)-\bar{h}(W,a,X))\mid A=a\right].

This proves the statement.
∎

B.3
Pathwise regularity of the smoothed target

The following condition justifies differentiation of the outcome-bridge
equation along regular parametric submodels. The same differentiation step is
used in the proximal influence function calculations in Sections D and G.2 of
Cui et al. (2024)
; the operator regularity condition used there is stated separately in their Appendix G.1.

For every regular one-dimensional parametric submodel

\{P_{\varepsilon}:|\varepsilon|<\delta\}\subset\mathcal{M}^{\dagger}
through

P_{0}
, with score
\dot{\ell}_{0}\in L_{2}^{0}(P_{0})
, there exists a function

\dot{h}_{0}\in L_{2}(P_{0,WX}\otimes F_{0})
such that

\int\left|\frac{h_{P_{\varepsilon}}(w,a,x)-h_{P_{0}}(w,a,x)}{\varepsilon}-\dot{h}_{0}(w,a,x)\right|^{2}\,d(P_{0,WX}\otimes F_{0})(w,a,x)\to 0

and

E_{0}\!\left[I\{|A-a_{0}|\leq\max(h,b)\}\left\{1+q_{0}(Z,A,X)^{2}\right\}\dot{h}_{0}(W,A,X)^{2}\right]<\infty,

and

E_{0}\!\left[\begin{aligned} &I\{|A-a_{0}|\leq\max(h,b)\}\{1+q_{0}(Z,A,X)^{2}\}\\[-1.99997pt]
&\quad\times\left|\frac{h_{P_{\varepsilon}}(W,A,X)-h_{0}(W,A,X)}{\varepsilon}-\dot{h}_{0}(W,A,X)\right|^{2}\end{aligned}\right]\to 0

as
\varepsilon\to 0
.

The first display gives the
L_{2}(P_{0,WX}\otimes F_{0})
derivative of the
outcome bridge. The second display gives the integrability of this derivative
under the weight used in the pathwise calculation, and the third gives
convergence under the same weight. These conditions permit differentiation
of the outcome-bridge equation after multiplication by the kernel-supported
weights and by
q_{0}
. The map
P\mapsto\theta_{P,h,b}(a_{0})
depends on
h_{P}
,

P_{WX}
, and
F_{P}
, but not on
q_{P}
. Consequently, no pathwise derivative
of
q_{P}
is required;
q_{0}
represents the derivative of
h_{P}
as an
observed-data score covariance.

Lemma 2
.

Let
P\in\mathcal{M}^{\dagger}
. Suppose that
r:\mathcal{A}\to\mathbb{R}
is bounded and measurable,

r(a)=0\qquad\text{if }|a-a_{0}|>\max(h,b),

and
u:\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{W}\times\mathcal{X}\to\mathbb{R}
is measurable with

E_{P}\{|r(A)q_{P}(Z,A,X)u(A,W,X)|\}<\infty

and

\int|r(a)|E_{P}\{|u(a,W,X)|\}\,dF_{P}(a)<\infty.

Then

\mathbb{E}_{P}\{r(A)q_{P}(Z,A,X)u(A,W,X)\}=\int r(a)\mathbb{E}_{P}\{u(a,W,X)\}\,dF_{P}(a).

Proof.

By iterated expectation,

\mathbb{E}_{P}\{r(A)q_{P}(Z,A,X)u(A,W,X)\}=\int r(a)\,\mathbb{E}_{P}\{q_{P}(Z,a,X)u(a,W,X)\mid A=a\}\,dF_{P}(a).

For
F_{P}
-almost every
a
,

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}_{P}\{q_{P}(Z,a,X)u(a,W,X)\mid A=a\}&=\mathbb{E}_{P}\!\left[u(a,W,X)\mathbb{E}_{P}\{q_{P}(Z,a,X)\mid W,A=a,X\}\,\middle|\,A=a\right]\\
&=\mathbb{E}_{P}\!\left[u(a,W,X)\frac{f_{P}(a)}{f_{P}(a\mid W,X)}\,\middle|\,A=a\right].\end{split}

For
F_{P}
-almost every
a
such that
r(a)\neq 0
, Assumption
C2
and

dP_{W,X\mid A=a}(w,x)=\frac{f_{P}(a\mid w,x)}{f_{P}(a)}\,dP_{W,X}(w,x),

give

\mathbb{E}_{P}\!\left[u(a,W,X)\frac{f_{P}(a)}{f_{P}(a\mid W,X)}\,\middle|\,A=a\right]=\iint u(a,w,x)\,dP_{W,X}(w,x)=\mathbb{E}_{P}\{u(a,W,X)\}.

Substituting this identity into the first display proves the claim.
∎

Lemma 3
.

Let
r:\mathcal{A}\to\mathbb{R}
be bounded and measurable and satisfy

r(a)=0\qquad\text{if }|a-a_{0}|>\max(h,b).

Define

\Psi_{r}(P):=\int r(a)\theta_{P}(a)\,dF_{P}(a).

Then, for every regular one-dimensional parametric submodel

\{P_{\varepsilon}:|\varepsilon|<\delta\}\subset\mathcal{M}^{\dagger}
through
P_{0}

with score
\dot{\ell}_{0}
,

\left.\frac{d}{d\varepsilon}\Psi_{r}(P_{\varepsilon})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}=\mathbb{E}_{0}\!\left[\left\{r(A)\xi_{0}(O)+\int r(\bar{a})\{h_{0}(W,\bar{a},X)-\theta_{0}(\bar{a})\}\,dF_{0}(\bar{a})\right\}\dot{\ell}_{0}(O)\right].

Proof.

Write
h_{\varepsilon}:=h_{P_{\varepsilon}}
,
\theta_{\varepsilon}:=\theta_{P_{\varepsilon}}
,
and let
P_{\varepsilon,WX}
and
F_{\varepsilon}
denote the

(W,X)
-marginal and
A
-marginal under
P_{\varepsilon}
, respectively.
Then

\Psi_{r}(P_{\varepsilon})=\int\!\!\int r(a)h_{\varepsilon}(w,a,x)\,dP_{\varepsilon,WX}(w,x)\,dF_{\varepsilon}(a).

Quadratic-mean differentiability of the submodel, the

L_{2}(P_{0,WX}\otimes F_{0})
-differentiability of
h_{\varepsilon}
, and the
Cauchy–Schwarz inequality give

\left.\frac{d}{d\varepsilon}\Psi_{r}(P_{\varepsilon})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}=I_{1}+I_{2}+I_{3},

where

I_{1}:=\int r(a)\mathbb{E}_{0}\{\dot{h}_{0}(W,a,X)\}\,dF_{0}(a),

I_{2}:=\mathbb{E}_{0}\!\left[\left\{\int r(\bar{a})h_{0}(W,\bar{a},X)\,dF_{0}(\bar{a})\right\}\dot{\ell}_{0}(O)\right],

and

I_{3}:=\mathbb{E}_{0}\{r(A)\theta_{0}(A)\dot{\ell}_{0}(O)\}.

It remains to identify
I_{1}
. Since
h_{\varepsilon}
satisfies

\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\{Y-h_{\varepsilon}(W,A,X)\mid Z,A,X\}=0,

for every
M>0
and every
\varepsilon
,

0=\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\!\left[r(A)\bigl[\{-M\vee q_{0}(Z,A,X)\}\wedge M\bigr]\{Y-h_{\varepsilon}(W,A,X)\}\right].

Assumption
C1
and the regularity condition in
Appendix
B.3
,
quadratic-mean differentiability, and the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality give, after
differentiation at
\varepsilon=0
and passage to the limit
M\to\infty
,

0=\mathbb{E}_{0}\!\left[r(A)q_{0}(Z,A,X)\{Y-h_{0}(W,A,X)\}\dot{\ell}_{0}(O)\right]-\mathbb{E}_{0}\{r(A)q_{0}(Z,A,X)\dot{h}_{0}(W,A,X)\}.

Therefore

\mathbb{E}_{0}\{r(A)q_{0}(Z,A,X)\dot{h}_{0}(W,A,X)\}=\mathbb{E}_{0}\!\left[r(A)q_{0}(Z,A,X)\{Y-h_{0}(W,A,X)\}\dot{\ell}_{0}(O)\right].

Applying the preceding lemma with
P=P_{0}
and
u(a,w,x)=\dot{h}_{0}(w,a,x)
,

\mathbb{E}_{0}\{r(A)q_{0}(Z,A,X)\dot{h}_{0}(W,A,X)\}=\int r(a)\mathbb{E}_{0}\{\dot{h}_{0}(W,a,X)\}\,dF_{0}(a)=I_{1}.

Hence

I_{1}=\mathbb{E}_{0}\!\left[r(A)q_{0}(Z,A,X)\{Y-h_{0}(W,A,X)\}\dot{\ell}_{0}(O)\right].

Combining
I_{1}
,
I_{2}
, and
I_{3}
,

\begin{split}\left.\frac{d}{d\varepsilon}\Psi_{r}(P_{\varepsilon})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}&=\mathbb{E}_{0}\!\Bigg[\Bigg\{r(A)q_{0}(Z,A,X)\{Y-h_{0}(W,A,X)\}+r(A)\theta_{0}(A)\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad+\int r(\bar{a})h_{0}(W,\bar{a},X)\,dF_{0}(\bar{a})\Bigg\}\dot{\ell}_{0}(O)\Bigg].\end{split}

(25)

Since

\xi_{0}(O)=q_{0}(Z,A,X)\{Y-h_{0}(W,A,X)\}+\theta_{0}(A),

and

\int r(\bar{a})\theta_{0}(\bar{a})\,dF_{0}(\bar{a})=\Psi_{r}(P_{0}),

while
\mathbb{E}_{0}\{\dot{\ell}_{0}(O)\}=0
, the preceding display is equivalent to

\left.\frac{d}{d\varepsilon}\Psi_{r}(P_{\varepsilon})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}=\mathbb{E}_{0}\!\left[\left\{r(A)\xi_{0}(O)+\int r(\bar{a})\{h_{0}(W,\bar{a},X)-\theta_{0}(\bar{a})\}\,dF_{0}(\bar{a})\right\}\dot{\ell}_{0}(O)\right],

which proves the claim.
∎

Proposition 1
.

Define

\gamma_{P,h,a_{0}}(a):=e_{1}^{\top}D_{P,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}w_{h,a_{0},1}(a)K_{h,a_{0}}(a)\,w_{h,a_{0},1}(a)^{\top}D_{P,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}M_{P,h,a_{0}}.

Then, for every regular one-dimensional parametric submodel

\{P_{\varepsilon}\}\subset\mathcal{M}^{\dagger}
through
P_{0}
,

\left.\frac{d}{d\varepsilon}\theta_{P_{\varepsilon},h}^{\mathrm{LL}}(a_{0})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}=\mathbb{E}_{0}\!\left[\left\{r_{0,h,a_{0}}(A)\xi_{0}(O)-\gamma_{0,h,a_{0}}(A)+\int r_{0,h,a_{0}}(\bar{a})\{h_{0}(W,\bar{a},X)-\theta_{0}(\bar{a})\}\,dF_{0}(\bar{a})\right\}\dot{\ell}_{0}(O)\right].

Proof.

Write

D_{\varepsilon}:=D_{P_{\varepsilon},h,a_{0},1},\qquad M_{\varepsilon}:=M_{P_{\varepsilon},h,a_{0}}.

Then

\theta_{P_{\varepsilon},h}^{\mathrm{LL}}(a_{0})=e_{1}^{\top}D_{\varepsilon}^{-1}M_{\varepsilon}.

Differentiating at
\varepsilon=0
and using the matrix identity

\left.\frac{d}{d\varepsilon}D_{\varepsilon}^{-1}\right|_{\varepsilon=0}=-D_{0}^{-1}\dot{D}_{0}D_{0}^{-1},

we obtain

\left.\frac{d}{d\varepsilon}\theta_{P_{\varepsilon},h}^{\mathrm{LL}}(a_{0})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}=-e_{1}^{\top}D_{0}^{-1}\dot{D}_{0}D_{0}^{-1}M_{0}+e_{1}^{\top}D_{0}^{-1}\dot{M}_{0}.

We first identify
\dot{D}_{0}
. Since

D_{\varepsilon}=P_{\varepsilon}\{w_{h,a_{0},1}(A)w_{h,a_{0},1}(A)^{\top}K_{h,a_{0}}(A)\},

the score identity gives

\dot{D}_{0}=\mathbb{E}_{0}\!\left[w_{h,a_{0},1}(A)w_{h,a_{0},1}(A)^{\top}K_{h,a_{0}}(A)\dot{\ell}_{0}(O)\right].

Therefore

\begin{split}-e_{1}^{\top}D_{0}^{-1}\dot{D}_{0}D_{0}^{-1}M_{0}&=-\mathbb{E}_{0}\!\left[e_{1}^{\top}D_{0}^{-1}w_{h,a_{0},1}(A)K_{h,a_{0}}(A)w_{h,a_{0},1}(A)^{\top}D_{0}^{-1}M_{0}\dot{\ell}_{0}(O)\right]\\
&=-\mathbb{E}_{0}\{\gamma_{0,h,a_{0}}(A)\dot{\ell}_{0}(O)\}\end{split}

(26)

We next identify
\dot{M}_{0}
. Each component of
M_{\varepsilon}
is a fixed-weight
functional of the form
\Psi_{r}(P_{\varepsilon})
with weight equal to a component of

w_{h,a_{0},1}(a)K_{h,a_{0}}(a)
. Applying Lemma
3
, we get

\dot{M}_{0}=\mathbb{E}_{0}\!\left[\left\{w_{h,a_{0},1}(A)K_{h,a_{0}}(A)\xi_{0}(O)+\int w_{h,a_{0},1}(\bar{a})K_{h,a_{0}}(\bar{a})\{h_{0}(W,\bar{a},X)-\theta_{0}(\bar{a})\}\,dF_{0}(\bar{a})\right\}\dot{\ell}_{0}(O)\right].

Multiplying by
e_{1}^{\top}D_{0}^{-1}
yields

e_{1}^{\top}D_{0}^{-1}\dot{M}_{0}=\mathbb{E}_{0}\!\left[\left\{r_{0,h,a_{0}}(A)\xi_{0}(O)+\int r_{0,h,a_{0}}(\bar{a})\{h_{0}(W,\bar{a},X)-\theta_{0}(\bar{a})\}\,dF_{0}(\bar{a})\right\}\dot{\ell}_{0}(O)\right].

Combining the last two displays proves the proposition.
∎

Proposition 2
.

Define

\gamma_{P,b,a_{0}}^{\prime\prime}(a):=2b^{-2}e_{3}^{\top}D_{P,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}w_{b,a_{0},2}(a)K_{b,a_{0}}(a)\,w_{b,a_{0},2}(a)^{\top}D_{P,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}M_{P,b,a_{0}}.

Then, for every regular one-dimensional parametric submodel

\{P_{\varepsilon}\}\subset\mathcal{M}^{\dagger}
through
P_{0}
,

\left.\frac{d}{d\varepsilon}\theta_{P_{\varepsilon},b}^{\prime\prime}(a_{0})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}=\mathbb{E}_{0}\!\left[\left\{s_{0,b,a_{0}}(A)\xi_{0}(O)-\gamma_{0,b,a_{0}}^{\prime\prime}(A)+\int s_{0,b,a_{0}}(\bar{a})\{h_{0}(W,\bar{a},X)-\theta_{0}(\bar{a})\}\,dF_{0}(\bar{a})\right\}\dot{\ell}_{0}(O)\right].

Proof.

Write

\widetilde{D}_{\varepsilon}:=D_{P_{\varepsilon},b,a_{0},2},\qquad\widetilde{M}_{\varepsilon}:=M_{P_{\varepsilon},b,a_{0}}.

Then

\theta_{P_{\varepsilon},b}^{\prime\prime}(a_{0})=2b^{-2}e_{3}^{\top}\widetilde{D}_{\varepsilon}^{-1}\widetilde{M}_{\varepsilon}.

Differentiating at
\varepsilon=0
,

\left.\frac{d}{d\varepsilon}\theta_{P_{\varepsilon},b}^{\prime\prime}(a_{0})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}=-2b^{-2}e_{3}^{\top}\widetilde{D}_{0}^{-1}\dot{\widetilde{D}}_{0}\widetilde{D}_{0}^{-1}\widetilde{M}_{0}+2b^{-2}e_{3}^{\top}\widetilde{D}_{0}^{-1}\dot{\widetilde{M}}_{0}.

The score identity gives

\dot{\widetilde{D}}_{0}=\mathbb{E}_{0}\!\left[w_{b,a_{0},2}(A)w_{b,a_{0},2}(A)^{\top}K_{b,a_{0}}(A)\dot{\ell}_{0}(O)\right],

so

-2b^{-2}e_{3}^{\top}\widetilde{D}_{0}^{-1}\dot{\widetilde{D}}_{0}\widetilde{D}_{0}^{-1}\widetilde{M}_{0}=-\mathbb{E}_{0}\{\gamma_{0,b,a_{0}}^{\prime\prime}(A)\dot{\ell}_{0}(O)\}.

Each component of
\widetilde{M}_{\varepsilon}
is again a fixed-weight functional,
now with weight equal to a component of
w_{b,a_{0},2}(a)K_{b,a_{0}}(a)
.
Applying Lemma
3
, we have

\dot{\widetilde{M}}_{0}=\mathbb{E}_{0}\!\left[\left\{w_{b,a_{0},2}(A)K_{b,a_{0}}(A)\xi_{0}(O)+\int w_{b,a_{0},2}(\bar{a})K_{b,a_{0}}(\bar{a})\{h_{0}(W,\bar{a},X)-\theta_{0}(\bar{a})\}\,dF_{0}(\bar{a})\right\}\dot{\ell}_{0}(O)\right].

Therefore

2b^{-2}e_{3}^{\top}\widetilde{D}_{0}^{-1}\dot{\widetilde{M}}_{0}=\mathbb{E}_{0}\!\left[\left\{s_{0,b,a_{0}}(A)\xi_{0}(O)+\int s_{0,b,a_{0}}(\bar{a})\{h_{0}(W,\bar{a},X)-\theta_{0}(\bar{a})\}\,dF_{0}(\bar{a})\right\}\dot{\ell}_{0}(O)\right].

Combining the last two displays proves the claim.
∎

Proposition 3
.

Define

U_{P,h,a_{0}}:=P\{\widetilde{w}_{h,a_{0},1}(A)K_{h,a_{0}}(A)\}

and

\gamma_{P,h,a_{0}}^{c}(a):=e_{1}^{\top}D_{P,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}\left[\widetilde{w}_{h,a_{0},1}(a)-w_{h,a_{0},1}(a)w_{h,a_{0},1}(a)^{\top}D_{P,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}U_{P,h,a_{0}}\right]K_{h,a_{0}}(a).

Then, for every regular one-dimensional parametric submodel

\{P_{\varepsilon}\}\subset\mathcal{M}^{\dagger}
through
P_{0}
,

\left.\frac{d}{d\varepsilon}c_{P_{\varepsilon},h,a_{0},2}\right|_{\varepsilon=0}=\mathbb{E}_{0}\{\gamma_{0,h,a_{0}}^{c}(A)\dot{\ell}_{0}(O)\}.

Moreover,

P\{\gamma_{P,h,a_{0}}^{c}(A)\}=0

for every
P\in\mathcal{M}^{\dagger}
.

Proof.

Write

D_{\varepsilon}:=D_{P_{\varepsilon},h,a_{0},1},\qquad U_{\varepsilon}:=U_{P_{\varepsilon},h,a_{0}}.

Then

c_{P_{\varepsilon},h,a_{0},2}=e_{1}^{\top}D_{\varepsilon}^{-1}U_{\varepsilon}.

Differentiating at
\varepsilon=0
,

\left.\frac{d}{d\varepsilon}c_{P_{\varepsilon},h,a_{0},2}\right|_{\varepsilon=0}=-e_{1}^{\top}D_{0}^{-1}\dot{D}_{0}D_{0}^{-1}U_{0}+e_{1}^{\top}D_{0}^{-1}\dot{U}_{0}.

Using the score identity,

\dot{D}_{0}=\mathbb{E}_{0}\!\left[w_{h,a_{0},1}(A)w_{h,a_{0},1}(A)^{\top}K_{h,a_{0}}(A)\dot{\ell}_{0}(O)\right]

and

\dot{U}_{0}=\mathbb{E}_{0}\!\left[\widetilde{w}_{h,a_{0},1}(A)K_{h,a_{0}}(A)\dot{\ell}_{0}(O)\right].

Therefore

\left.\frac{d}{d\varepsilon}c_{P_{\varepsilon},h,a_{0},2}\right|_{\varepsilon=0}=\mathbb{E}_{0}\!\left[e_{1}^{\top}D_{0}^{-1}\left\{\widetilde{w}_{h,a_{0},1}(A)-w_{h,a_{0},1}(A)w_{h,a_{0},1}(A)^{\top}D_{0}^{-1}U_{0}\right\}K_{h,a_{0}}(A)\dot{\ell}_{0}(O)\right],

which is the desired representation.

For the mean,

P\{\gamma_{P,h,a_{0}}^{c}(A)\}=e_{1}^{\top}D_{P,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}\left[U_{P,h,a_{0}}-D_{P,h,a_{0},1}D_{P,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}U_{P,h,a_{0}}\right]=0.

∎

Proof of Theorem
2
.

Recall

\theta_{P,h,b}(a_{0})=\theta_{P,h}^{\mathrm{LL}}(a_{0})-\frac{1}{2}h^{2}c_{P,h,a_{0},2}\theta_{P,b}^{\prime\prime}(a_{0}).

Differentiating along a regular one-dimensional parametric submodel

\{P_{\varepsilon}\}\subset\mathcal{M}^{\dagger}
through
P_{0}
,

\left.\frac{d}{d\varepsilon}\theta_{P_{\varepsilon},h,b}(a_{0})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}=\left.\frac{d}{d\varepsilon}\theta_{P_{\varepsilon},h}^{\mathrm{LL}}(a_{0})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}-\frac{1}{2}h^{2}\left\{\left.\frac{d}{d\varepsilon}c_{P_{\varepsilon},h,a_{0},2}\right|_{\varepsilon=0}\theta_{0,b}^{\prime\prime}(a_{0})+c_{0,h,a_{0},2}\left.\frac{d}{d\varepsilon}\theta_{P_{\varepsilon},b}^{\prime\prime}(a_{0})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}\right\}.

Applying Propositions
1
–
3
gives

\begin{split}&\left.\frac{d}{d\varepsilon}\theta_{P_{\varepsilon},h,b}(a_{0})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}\\
&=\mathbb{E}_{0}\!\left[\left\{r_{0,h,a_{0}}(A)\xi_{0}(O)-\gamma_{0,h,a_{0}}(A)+\int r_{0,h,a_{0}}(\bar{a})\{h_{0}(W,\bar{a},X)-\theta_{0}(\bar{a})\}\,dF_{0}(\bar{a})\right\}\dot{\ell}_{0}(O)\right]\\
&\qquad\qquad-\frac{1}{2}h^{2}c_{0,h,a_{0},2}\,\mathbb{E}_{0}\!\left[\left\{s_{0,b,a_{0}}(A)\xi_{0}(O)-\gamma_{0,b,a_{0}}^{\prime\prime}(A)+\int s_{0,b,a_{0}}(\bar{a})\{h_{0}(W,\bar{a},X)-\theta_{0}(\bar{a})\}\,dF_{0}(\bar{a})\right\}\dot{\ell}_{0}(O)\right]\\
&\qquad\qquad-\frac{1}{2}h^{2}\theta_{0,b}^{\prime\prime}(a_{0})\,\mathbb{E}_{0}\{\gamma_{0,h,a_{0}}^{c}(A)\dot{\ell}_{0}(O)\}.\end{split}

(27)

Collecting terms, we get

\begin{split}&\left.\frac{d}{d\varepsilon}\theta_{P_{\varepsilon},h,b}(a_{0})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}\\
&\qquad=\mathbb{E}_{0}\!\left[\left\{\left(r_{0,h,a_{0}}(A)-\frac{1}{2}h^{2}c_{0,h,a_{0},2}s_{0,b,a_{0}}(A)\right)\xi_{0}(O)\right.\right.\\
&\left.\left.\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\left(\gamma_{0,h,a_{0}}(A)-\frac{1}{2}h^{2}c_{0,h,a_{0},2}\gamma_{0,b,a_{0}}^{\prime\prime}(A)+\frac{1}{2}h^{2}\theta_{0,b}^{\prime\prime}(a_{0})\gamma_{0,h,a_{0}}^{c}(A)\right)\right.\right.\\
&\left.\left.\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\int\left(r_{0,h,a_{0}}(\bar{a})-\frac{1}{2}h^{2}c_{0,h,a_{0},2}s_{0,b,a_{0}}(\bar{a})\right)\{h_{0}(W,\bar{a},X)-\theta_{0}(\bar{a})\}\,dF_{0}(\bar{a})\right\}\dot{\ell}_{0}(O)\right].\end{split}

(28)

By definition of
\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}
and
\gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}
,

\left.\frac{d}{d\varepsilon}\theta_{P_{\varepsilon},h,b}(a_{0})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}=\mathbb{E}_{0}\{\varphi_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(O)\dot{\ell}_{0}(O)\}.

Thus
\varphi_{0,h,b,a_{0}}
is a pathwise gradient of
\theta_{P,h,b}(a_{0})
at
P_{0}
.

It remains to verify that
\varphi_{0,h,b,a_{0}}
has mean zero. First, because

\mathbb{E}_{0}\{\xi_{0}(O)\mid A\}=\theta_{0}(A)
,

\mathbb{E}_{0}\{\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(A)\xi_{0}(O)\}=\mathbb{E}_{0}\{\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(A)\theta_{0}(A)\}=\theta_{0,h,b}(a_{0}).

Second,

\mathbb{E}_{0}\!\left[\int\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(\bar{a})\{h_{0}(W,\bar{a},X)-\theta_{0}(\bar{a})\}\,dF_{0}(\bar{a})\right]=\int\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(\bar{a})\{\theta_{0}(\bar{a})-\theta_{0}(\bar{a})\}\,dF_{0}(\bar{a})=0.

Third,

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}_{0}\{\gamma_{0,h,a_{0}}(A)\}&=e_{1}^{\top}D_{0,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}P_{0}\{w_{h,a_{0},1}(A)K_{h,a_{0}}(A)w_{h,a_{0},1}(A)^{\top}\}D_{0,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}M_{0,h,a_{0}}\\
&=e_{1}^{\top}D_{0,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}M_{0,h,a_{0}},\end{split}

which is equal to
\theta_{0,h}^{\mathrm{LL}}(a_{0})
.
Likewise,

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}_{0}\{\gamma_{0,b,a_{0}}^{\prime\prime}(A)\}&=2b^{-2}e_{3}^{\top}D_{0,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}P_{0}\{w_{b,a_{0},2}(A)K_{b,a_{0}}(A)w_{b,a_{0},2}(A)^{\top}\}D_{0,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}M_{0,b,a_{0}}\\
&=2b^{-2}e_{3}^{\top}D_{0,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}M_{0,b,a_{0}}\\
&=\theta_{0,b}^{\prime\prime}(a_{0}),\end{split}

and, by Proposition
3
,

\mathbb{E}_{0}\{\gamma_{0,h,a_{0}}^{c}(A)\}=0.

Therefore

\mathbb{E}_{0}\{\gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(A)\}=\theta_{0,h}^{\mathrm{LL}}(a_{0})-\frac{1}{2}h^{2}c_{0,h,a_{0},2}\theta_{0,b}^{\prime\prime}(a_{0})+\frac{1}{2}h^{2}\theta_{0,b}^{\prime\prime}(a_{0})\cdot 0=\theta_{0,h,b}(a_{0}).

Combining the three mean identities,

\mathbb{E}_{0}\{\varphi_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(O)\}=0.

Assumption
C1
, the regularity condition in
Appendix
B.3
, and the
bounded support of the fixed kernel weights imply

\varphi_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\in L_{2}^{0}(P_{0}).

The derivative identity and the preceding mean identity prove the theorem.
∎

B.4
Equivalent representations of the cross-fitted estimator

Lemma 4
.

For every
a_{0}
, the two right-hand sides in
(
6
) and the right-hand side in
(
7
) are equal.

Proof.

For each fold
k
, the definition of
\hat{m}_{n,k}^{(-k)}
gives

\displaystyle P_{n,k}\{\Gamma_{n,h_{n},b_{n},a_{0}}(A)\hat{m}_{n,k}^{(-k)}(A)\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n_{k}}\sum_{j\in I_{k}}\Gamma_{n,h_{n},b_{n},a_{0}}(A_{j})\left\{\frac{1}{n_{k}}\sum_{i\in I_{k}}\hat{h}_{n}^{(-k)}(W_{i},A_{j},X_{i})\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n_{k}^{2}}\sum_{i\in I_{k}}\sum_{j\in I_{k}}\Gamma_{n,h_{n},b_{n},a_{0}}(A_{j})\hat{h}_{n}^{(-k)}(W_{i},A_{j},X_{i})

\displaystyle=(Q_{n,k}^{WX}\times F_{n,k})\{\Gamma_{n,h_{n},b_{n},a_{0}}(A)\hat{h}_{n}^{(-k)}(W,A,X)\}.

Since

\hat{\xi}_{n}^{(-k)}=\hat{\xi}_{n,1}^{(-k)}+\hat{m}_{n,k}^{(-k)}
, it follows that

\displaystyle P_{n,k}\{\Gamma_{n,h_{n},b_{n},a_{0}}(A)\hat{\xi}_{n}^{(-k)}(O)\}={}

\displaystyle P_{n,k}\{\Gamma_{n,h_{n},b_{n},a_{0}}(A)\hat{\xi}_{n,1}^{(-k)}(O)\}

\displaystyle+(Q_{n,k}^{WX}\times F_{n,k})\{\Gamma_{n,h_{n},b_{n},a_{0}}(A)\hat{h}_{n}^{(-k)}(W,A,X)\}.

This proves the equality of the two right-hand sides in
(
6
). Moreover,

\displaystyle\frac{n_{k}}{n}

\displaystyle P_{n,k}\{\Gamma_{n,h_{n},b_{n},a_{0}}(A)\hat{\xi}_{n,1}^{(-k)}(O)\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in I_{k}}\Gamma_{n,h_{n},b_{n},a_{0}}(A_{i})\hat{q}_{n}^{(-k)}(Z_{i},A_{i},X_{i})\{Y_{i}-\hat{h}_{n}^{(-k)}(W_{i},A_{i},X_{i})\},

\displaystyle\frac{n_{k}}{n}

\displaystyle P_{n,k}\{\Gamma_{n,h_{n},b_{n},a_{0}}(A)\hat{m}_{n,k}^{(-k)}(A)\}=\frac{1}{nn_{k}}\sum_{i\in I_{k}}\sum_{j\in I_{k}}\Gamma_{n,h_{n},b_{n},a_{0}}(A_{j})\hat{h}_{n}^{(-k)}(W_{i},A_{j},X_{i}).

Summing these two identities over
k=1,\ldots,K
yields
(
7
).
∎

Appendix C
Auxiliary results for debiased inference

C.1
Multiple-
a
inference

Fix an exposure value
a_{0}
in the interior of the support of
A
.
Let
(q_{\infty},h_{\infty})
be a limiting nuisance pair and define

m_{\infty}(a):=E_{0}\{h_{\infty}(W,a,X)\},

and

\xi_{\infty}(O):=q_{\infty}(Z,A,X)\{Y-h_{\infty}(W,A,X)\}+m_{\infty}(A).

Under Assumptions
A
,
B
, and
F
, the proximal double-robust identity implies that

E_{0}\{\xi_{\infty}(O)\mid A=a\}=\theta_{0}(a)

for
f_{A}
-almost every
a\in B_{\delta_{1}}(a_{0})
.

For exposure value
a
, define

\sigma_{0}^{2}(a):=E_{0}\left([\xi_{\infty}(O)-\theta_{0}(A)]^{2}\mid A=a\right).

Also define

\displaystyle\phi_{\infty,a_{0}}(O):={}

\displaystyle\Gamma_{0,h_{n},b_{n},a_{0}}(A)\xi_{\infty}(O)-\gamma_{0,h_{n},b_{n},a_{0}}(A)

\displaystyle+\int\Gamma_{0,h_{n},b_{n},a_{0}}(\bar{a})\{h_{\infty}(W,\bar{a},X)-m_{\infty}(\bar{a})\}\,dF_{0}(\bar{a}),

and

\displaystyle\psi_{n,a_{0}}^{sm}(O):={}

\displaystyle\Gamma_{0,h_{n},b_{n},a_{0}}(A)\theta_{0}(A)-\gamma_{0,h_{n},b_{n},a_{0}}(A)

\displaystyle+\int\Gamma_{0,h_{n},b_{n},a_{0}}(\bar{a})\{h_{\infty}(W,\bar{a},X)-m_{\infty}(\bar{a})\}\,dF_{0}(\bar{a}).

For
j\geq 0
, define kernel moments

c_{j}:=\int u^{j}K(u)\,du,\qquad c_{j}^{\ast}:=\int u^{j}K(u)^{2}\,du,\qquad c_{j,\tau}^{\ast}:=\int u^{j}K(u)K(\tau u)\,du,

and matrices

S_{2}:=\begin{pmatrix}c_{0}&c_{1}\\
c_{1}&c_{2}\end{pmatrix},\qquad S_{3}:=\begin{pmatrix}c_{0}&c_{1}&c_{2}\\
c_{1}&c_{2}&c_{3}\\
c_{2}&c_{3}&c_{4}\end{pmatrix}.

Let

V_{K,\tau}:=\int\left\{K(u)-\tau^{3}c_{2}\frac{(\tau u)^{2}-c_{2}}{c_{4}-c_{2}^{2}}K(\tau u)\right\}^{2}\,du.

The assumptions used in the results below are Assumptions
A
,
B
, and
D
–
F
for the pointwise and finite-dimensional statements, and Assumptions
A
,
B
,
D
, and
G
for the uniform statements.

C.2
Remainder decomposition

Following the decomposition in Section D of

Takatsu and Westling (2025)
, write

\Gamma_{n}(a):=\Gamma_{n,h_{n},b_{n},a_{0}}(a),\qquad\Gamma_{0}(a):=\Gamma_{0,h_{n},b_{n},a_{0}}(a),\qquad\gamma_{0}(a):=\gamma_{0,h_{n},b_{n},a_{0}}(a),

and recall that
\tau_{n}=h_{n}/b_{n}
. Let
Q_{0}^{WX}
denote the

P_{0}
-law of
(W,X)
. Whenever
P_{0}
is applied to a
sample-dependent function, the observed sample is held fixed and
P_{0}

denotes integration with respect to an independent observation

O\sim P_{0}
.

Define

R_{n,1}(a_{0}):=\theta_{0,h_{n},b_{n}}(a_{0})-\theta_{0}(a_{0}).

The nuisance empirical-process remainder is

\displaystyle R_{n,2}(a_{0}):=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\frac{n_{k}}{n}(P_{n,k}-P_{0})\Bigg[

\displaystyle\Gamma_{0}(A)\left\{\hat{\xi}_{n,1}^{(-k)}(O)+S_{\hat{h}_{n}^{(-k)}}(A)\right\}+\int\Gamma_{0}(\bar{a})\hat{h}_{n}^{(-k)}(W,\bar{a},X)\,dF_{0}(\bar{a})

\displaystyle-\Gamma_{0}(A)\xi_{\infty}(O)-\int\Gamma_{0}(\bar{a})h_{\infty}(W,\bar{a},X)\,dF_{0}(\bar{a})\Bigg].

The empirical-process remainder for the estimated local-polynomial weight is

\displaystyle R_{n,3}(a_{0}):=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\frac{n_{k}}{n}(P_{n,k}-P_{0})\Bigg[

\displaystyle\{\Gamma_{n}(A)-\Gamma_{0}(A)\}\left\{\hat{\xi}_{n,1}^{(-k)}(O)+S_{\hat{h}_{n}^{(-k)}}(A)\right\}

\displaystyle+\int\{\Gamma_{n}(\bar{a})-\Gamma_{0}(\bar{a})\}\hat{h}_{n}^{(-k)}(W,\bar{a},X)\,dF_{0}(\bar{a})\Bigg].

The proximal doubly robust remainder is

\displaystyle R_{n,4}(a_{0}):=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\frac{n_{k}}{n}\int\Gamma_{n}(a)E_{0}\Big[

\displaystyle\{\hat{q}_{n}^{(-k)}(Z,a,X)-q_{0}(Z,a,X)\}

\displaystyle\times\{h_{0}(W,a,X)-\hat{h}_{n}^{(-k)}(W,a,X)\}\,\Big|\,A=a\Big]\,dF_{0}(a).

The product empirical-measure remainder is

\displaystyle R_{n,5}(a_{0}):=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\frac{n_{k}}{n}\iint

\displaystyle\Gamma_{n}(a)\hat{h}_{n}^{(-k)}(w,a,x)d(Q_{n,k}^{WX}-Q_{0}^{WX})(w,x)\,d(F_{n,k}-F_{0})(a).

Finally, define the local-polynomial matrix remainder by

\displaystyle R_{n,6}(a_{0}):={}

\displaystyle e_{1}^{\top}D_{0,h_{n},a_{0},1}^{-1}(D_{0,h_{n},a_{0},1}-D_{n,h_{n},a_{0},1})(D_{n,h_{n},a_{0},1}^{-1}-D_{0,h_{n},a_{0},1}^{-1})

\displaystyle\times P_{0}\!\left\{w_{h_{n},a_{0},1}(A)K_{h_{n},a_{0}}(A)\theta_{0}(A)\right\}

\displaystyle-c_{0,h_{n},a_{0},2}\tau_{n}^{2}e_{3}^{\top}D_{0,b_{n},a_{0},2}^{-1}(D_{0,b_{n},a_{0},2}-D_{n,b_{n},a_{0},2})(D_{n,b_{n},a_{0},2}^{-1}-D_{0,b_{n},a_{0},2}^{-1})

\displaystyle\times P_{0}\!\left\{w_{b_{n},a_{0},2}(A)K_{b_{n},a_{0}}(A)\theta_{0}(A)\right\}

\displaystyle-\tau_{n}^{2}e_{1}^{\top}(D_{n,h_{n},a_{0},1}^{-1}-D_{0,h_{n},a_{0},1}^{-1})(P_{n}-P_{0})\left\{\widetilde{w}_{h_{n},a_{0},1}(A)K_{h_{n},a_{0}}(A)\right\}

\displaystyle\times e_{3}^{\top}D_{0,b_{n},a_{0},2}^{-1}P_{0}\!\left\{w_{b_{n},a_{0},2}(A)K_{b_{n},a_{0}}(A)\theta_{0}(A)\right\}

\displaystyle-\tau_{n}^{2}e_{1}^{\top}D_{0,h_{n},a_{0},1}^{-1}(D_{0,h_{n},a_{0},1}-D_{n,h_{n},a_{0},1})(D_{n,h_{n},a_{0},1}^{-1}-D_{0,h_{n},a_{0},1}^{-1})

\displaystyle\times P_{0}\!\left\{\widetilde{w}_{h_{n},a_{0},1}(A)K_{h_{n},a_{0}}(A)\right\}e_{3}^{\top}D_{0,b_{n},a_{0},2}^{-1}P_{0}\!\left\{w_{b_{n},a_{0},2}(A)K_{b_{n},a_{0}}(A)\theta_{0}(A)\right\}

\displaystyle-\tau_{n}^{2}(c_{n,h_{n},a_{0},2}-c_{0,h_{n},a_{0},2})e_{3}^{\top}(D_{n,b_{n},a_{0},2}^{-1}-D_{0,b_{n},a_{0},2}^{-1})

\displaystyle\times P_{0}\!\left\{w_{b_{n},a_{0},2}(A)K_{b_{n},a_{0}}(A)\theta_{0}(A)\right\}.

Lemma 5
.

For all sufficiently large
n
, on the event that the empirical moment
matrices are nonsingular,

\hat{\theta}_{n}^{DB,cf}(a_{0})-\theta_{0}(a_{0})=P_{n}\phi_{\infty,a_{0}}+\sum_{j=1}^{6}R_{n,j}(a_{0}).

Proof.

Recall
P_{0}\phi_{\infty,a_{0}}=0
.
We now expand the estimator fold by fold. Fix
k
. Bilinearity of
product measures gives

\displaystyle Q_{n,k}^{WX}\times F_{n,k}={}

\displaystyle Q_{0}^{WX}\times F_{0}+Q_{0}^{WX}\times(F_{n,k}-F_{0})

\displaystyle+(Q_{n,k}^{WX}-Q_{0}^{WX})\times F_{0}+(Q_{n,k}^{WX}-Q_{0}^{WX})\times(F_{n,k}-F_{0}).

The first three components satisfy

\displaystyle(Q_{0}^{WX}\times F_{0})\{\Gamma_{n}(A)\hat{h}_{n}^{(-k)}(W,A,X)\}=P_{0}\left[\Gamma_{n}(A)S_{\hat{h}_{n}^{(-k)}}(A)\right],

\displaystyle\{Q_{0}^{WX}\times(F_{n,k}-F_{0})\}\{\Gamma_{n}(A)\hat{h}_{n}^{(-k)}(W,A,X)\}=(P_{n,k}-P_{0})\left[\Gamma_{n}(A)S_{\hat{h}_{n}^{(-k)}}(A)\right],

and

\displaystyle\{(Q_{n,k}^{WX}-Q_{0}^{WX})\times F_{0}\}\{\Gamma_{n}(A)\hat{h}_{n}^{(-k)}(W,A,X)\}

\displaystyle=(P_{n,k}-P_{0})\left[\int\Gamma_{n}(\bar{a})\hat{h}_{n}^{(-k)}(W,\bar{a},X)\,dF_{0}(\bar{a})\right].

It follows that the
k
th fold contribution to the estimator equals

\displaystyle P_{n,k}\left\{\Gamma_{n}(A)\hat{\xi}_{n,1}^{(-k)}(O)\right\}+(Q_{n,k}^{WX}\times F_{n,k})\left\{\Gamma_{n}(A)\hat{h}_{n}^{(-k)}(W,A,X)\right\}

\displaystyle=(P_{n,k}-P_{0})\Bigg[\Gamma_{n}(A)\left\{\hat{\xi}_{n,1}^{(-k)}(O)+S_{\hat{h}_{n}^{(-k)}}(A)\right\}+\int\Gamma_{n}(\bar{a})\hat{h}_{n}^{(-k)}(W,\bar{a},X)\,dF_{0}(\bar{a})\Bigg]

\displaystyle+P_{0}\left[\Gamma_{n}(A)\left\{\hat{\xi}_{n,1}^{(-k)}(O)+S_{\hat{h}_{n}^{(-k)}}(A)\right\}\right]

\displaystyle+\iint\Gamma_{n}(a)\hat{h}_{n}^{(-k)}(w,a,x)\,d(Q_{n,k}^{WX}-Q_{0}^{WX})(w,x)\,d(F_{n,k}-F_{0})(a).

Multiplying by
n_{k}/n
and summing over
k
, the last line gives

R_{n,5}(a_{0})
.

Since
P_{0}\phi_{\infty,a_{0}}=0
, we may write

\displaystyle P_{n}\phi_{\infty,a_{0}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\frac{n_{k}}{n}(P_{n,k}-P_{0})\Bigg[\Gamma_{0}(A)\xi_{\infty}(O)+\int\Gamma_{0}(\bar{a})h_{\infty}(W,\bar{a},X)\,dF_{0}(\bar{a})\Bigg]-(P_{n}-P_{0})\{\gamma_{0}(A)\}.

Indeed, the omitted term

\int\Gamma_{0}(\bar{a})m_{\infty}(\bar{a})\,dF_{0}(\bar{a})

is constant and is therefore cancelled by
P_{n}-P_{0}
.

Subtracting this representation from the preceding fold expansion and then
adding and subtracting the same fold-specific expression with
\Gamma_{n}

replaced by
\Gamma_{0}
gives

\displaystyle\hat{\theta}_{n}^{DB,cf}(a_{0})-\theta_{0}(a_{0})-P_{n}\phi_{\infty,a_{0}}

\displaystyle=R_{n,2}(a_{0})+R_{n,3}(a_{0})+R_{n,5}(a_{0})

\displaystyle+\sum_{k=1}^{K}\frac{n_{k}}{n}P_{0}\left[\Gamma_{n}(A)\left\{\hat{\xi}_{n,1}^{(-k)}(O)+S_{\hat{h}_{n}^{(-k)}}(A)\right\}\right]

\displaystyle+(P_{n}-P_{0})\{\gamma_{0}(A)\}-\theta_{0}(a_{0}).

By the proximal product-bias identity in
Theorem
1
, applied pathwise with

\bar{q}=\hat{q}_{n}^{(-k)},\qquad\bar{h}=\hat{h}_{n}^{(-k)},

we have, for
F_{0}
-almost every
a
,

\displaystyle E_{0}\left[\hat{\xi}_{n,1}^{(-k)}(O)+S_{\hat{h}_{n}^{(-k)}}(A)\,\middle|\,A=a\right]-\theta_{0}(a)

\displaystyle=E_{0}\Big[\{\hat{q}_{n}^{(-k)}(Z,a,X)-q_{0}(Z,a,X)\}\{h_{0}(W,a,X)-\hat{h}_{n}^{(-k)}(W,a,X)\}\,\Big|\,A=a\Big].

Consequently,

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{K}\frac{n_{k}}{n}P_{0}\left[\Gamma_{n}(A)\left\{\hat{\xi}_{n,1}^{(-k)}(O)+S_{\hat{h}_{n}^{(-k)}}(A)\right\}\right]=P_{0}\{\Gamma_{n}(A)\theta_{0}(A)\}+R_{n,4}(a_{0}).

It follows that

\displaystyle\hat{\theta}_{n}^{DB,cf}(a_{0})-\theta_{0}(a_{0})-P_{n}\phi_{\infty,a_{0}}

\displaystyle=R_{n,2}(a_{0})+R_{n,3}(a_{0})+R_{n,4}(a_{0})+R_{n,5}(a_{0})

\displaystyle+(P_{n}-P_{0})\{\gamma_{0}(A)\}+P_{0}\{\Gamma_{n}(A)\theta_{0}(A)\}-\theta_{0}(a_{0}).

Adding and subtracting

P_{0}\{\Gamma_{0}(A)\theta_{0}(A)\}=\theta_{0,h_{n},b_{n}}(a_{0})

shows that the last line equals

R_{n,1}(a_{0})+(P_{n}-P_{0})\{\gamma_{0}(A)\}+P_{0}\!\left[\{\Gamma_{n}(A)-\Gamma_{0}(A)\}\theta_{0}(A)\right].

It remains to identify the last two terms. Direct substitution of the
definitions of
\Gamma_{n},\Gamma_{0}
, and
\gamma_{0}
gives

\displaystyle P_{0}\!\left[\{\Gamma_{n}(A)-\Gamma_{0}(A)\}\theta_{0}(A)\right]

\displaystyle=e_{1}^{\top}(D_{n,h_{n},a_{0},1}^{-1}-D_{0,h_{n},a_{0},1}^{-1})P_{0}\!\left\{w_{h_{n},a_{0},1}(A)K_{h_{n},a_{0}}(A)\theta_{0}(A)\right\}

\displaystyle-\tau_{n}^{2}\left\{c_{n,h_{n},a_{0},2}e_{3}^{\top}D_{n,b_{n},a_{0},2}^{-1}-c_{0,h_{n},a_{0},2}e_{3}^{\top}D_{0,b_{n},a_{0},2}^{-1}\right\}P_{0}\!\left\{w_{b_{n},a_{0},2}(A)K_{b_{n},a_{0}}(A)\theta_{0}(A)\right\},

whereas

\displaystyle(P_{n}-P_{0})\{\gamma_{0}(A)\}

\displaystyle=e_{1}^{\top}D_{0,h_{n},a_{0},1}^{-1}(D_{n,h_{n},a_{0},1}-D_{0,h_{n},a_{0},1})D_{0,h_{n},a_{0},1}^{-1}P_{0}\!\left\{w_{h_{n},a_{0},1}(A)K_{h_{n},a_{0}}(A)\theta_{0}(A)\right\}

\displaystyle-c_{0,h_{n},a_{0},2}\tau_{n}^{2}e_{3}^{\top}D_{0,b_{n},a_{0},2}^{-1}(D_{n,b_{n},a_{0},2}-D_{0,b_{n},a_{0},2})D_{0,b_{n},a_{0},2}^{-1}P_{0}\!\left\{w_{b_{n},a_{0},2}(A)K_{b_{n},a_{0}}(A)\theta_{0}(A)\right\}

\displaystyle+\tau_{n}^{2}e_{1}^{\top}D_{0,h_{n},a_{0},1}^{-1}\Big[(P_{n}-P_{0})\{\widetilde{w}_{h_{n},a_{0},1}(A)K_{h_{n},a_{0}}(A)\}

\displaystyle-(D_{n,h_{n},a_{0},1}-D_{0,h_{n},a_{0},1})D_{0,h_{n},a_{0},1}^{-1}P_{0}\{\widetilde{w}_{h_{n},a_{0},1}(A)K_{h_{n},a_{0}}(A)\}\Big]

\displaystyle\times e_{3}^{\top}D_{0,b_{n},a_{0},2}^{-1}P_{0}\!\left\{w_{b_{n},a_{0},2}(A)K_{b_{n},a_{0}}(A)\theta_{0}(A)\right\}.

Using

D_{n}^{-1}-D_{0}^{-1}=D_{0}^{-1}(D_{0}-D_{n})D_{n}^{-1}

for each of the two local-polynomial moment matrices, together with

\displaystyle c_{n,h_{n},a_{0},2}-c_{0,h_{n},a_{0},2}={}

\displaystyle e_{1}^{\top}(D_{n,h_{n},a_{0},1}^{-1}-D_{0,h_{n},a_{0},1}^{-1})P_{n}\{\widetilde{w}_{h_{n},a_{0},1}K_{h_{n},a_{0}}\}

\displaystyle+e_{1}^{\top}D_{0,h_{n},a_{0},1}^{-1}(P_{n}-P_{0})\{\widetilde{w}_{h_{n},a_{0},1}K_{h_{n},a_{0}}\},

and

\displaystyle e_{1}^{\top}D_{0,h_{n},a_{0},1}^{-1}\Big[(P_{n}-P_{0})\{\widetilde{w}_{h_{n},a_{0},1}K_{h_{n},a_{0}}\}-(D_{n,h_{n},a_{0},1}-D_{0,h_{n},a_{0},1})D_{0,h_{n},a_{0},1}^{-1}P_{0}\{\widetilde{w}_{h_{n},a_{0},1}K_{h_{n},a_{0}}\}\Big]

\displaystyle-(c_{n,h_{n},a_{0},2}-c_{0,h_{n},a_{0},2})

\displaystyle=-e_{1}^{\top}(D_{n,h_{n},a_{0},1}^{-1}-D_{0,h_{n},a_{0},1}^{-1})(P_{n}-P_{0})\{\widetilde{w}_{h_{n},a_{0},1}K_{h_{n},a_{0}}\}

\displaystyle-e_{1}^{\top}D_{0,h_{n},a_{0},1}^{-1}(D_{0,h_{n},a_{0},1}-D_{n,h_{n},a_{0},1})(D_{n,h_{n},a_{0},1}^{-1}-D_{0,h_{n},a_{0},1}^{-1})P_{0}\{\widetilde{w}_{h_{n},a_{0},1}K_{h_{n},a_{0}}\},

and collecting terms yields

(P_{n}-P_{0})\{\gamma_{0}(A)\}+P_{0}\!\left[\{\Gamma_{n}(A)-\Gamma_{0}(A)\}\theta_{0}(A)\right]=R_{n,6}(a_{0}).

Combining the preceding identities proves the claimed decomposition.
∎

Lemma 6
.

If conditions
D1
,
D2
, and
E1
(b)
hold, then

D_{0,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}=f_{A}(a_{0})^{-1}S_{2}^{-1}+O(h),\quad c_{0,h,a_{0},2}=c_{2}+O(h)

as
h\to 0
, and

D_{0,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}=f_{A}(a_{0})^{-1}S_{3}^{-1}+O(b)

as
b\to 0
.

If conditions
D1
,
D2
, and
G5
(ii)
hold, then

\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left\|D_{0,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}-f_{A}(a_{0})^{-1}S_{2}^{-1}\right\|_{\infty}=O(h),

\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\bigl|c_{0,h,a_{0},2}-c_{2}\bigr|=O(h),

and

\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left\|D_{0,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}-f_{A}(a_{0})^{-1}S_{3}^{-1}\right\|_{\infty}=O(b)

as
h,b\to 0
.

Proof is given in Lemma 4 of
Takatsu and Westling (2025)
, so we omit the technical details here.

Lemma 7
.

Fix
a_{0}
in the interior of the support of
A
. Assume Assumptions
A
,
B
, and
D
–
F
. Then

(nh_{n})^{1/2}P_{n}\left[\Gamma_{0,h_{n},b_{n},a_{0}}(A)\{\xi_{\infty}(O)-\theta_{0}(A)\}\right]\rightsquigarrow N\left(0,\frac{V_{K,\tau}\sigma_{0}^{2}(a_{0})}{f_{A}(a_{0})}\right).

Proof.

Write

V_{n,i}:=h_{n}^{1/2}\Gamma_{0,h_{n},b_{n},a_{0}}(A_{i})\{\xi_{\infty}(O_{i})-\theta_{0}(A_{i})\},\qquad i=1,\ldots,n.

Then

(nh_{n})^{1/2}P_{n}\left[\Gamma_{0,h_{n},b_{n},a_{0}}(A)\{\xi_{\infty}(O)-\theta_{0}(A)\}\right]=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}V_{n,i}.

First, we have

E_{0}(V_{n,i})=h_{n}^{1/2}E_{0}\left[\Gamma_{0,h_{n},b_{n},a_{0}}(A)E_{0}\{\xi_{\infty}(O)-\theta_{0}(A)\mid A\}\right]=0.

Next, since

E_{0}\left([\xi_{\infty}(O)-\theta_{0}(A)]^{2}\mid A=a\right)=\sigma_{0}^{2}(a),

we have

E_{0}(V_{n,i}^{2})=h_{n}\int\Gamma_{0,h_{n},b_{n},a_{0}}(a)^{2}\sigma_{0}^{2}(a)f_{A}(a)\,da.

By Assumptions
E1
,
F2
, and
F3
,

\sup_{|a-a_{0}|\leq\max(h_{n},b_{n})}E_{0}\left[|\xi_{\infty}(O)-\theta_{0}(A)|^{2+\delta_{2}}\mid A=a\right]\leq C

for all sufficiently large
n
. Lemma
6
gives

|r_{0,h_{n},a_{0}}(a)|\leq Ch_{n}^{-1}I(|a-a_{0}|\leq h_{n}),\qquad|s_{0,b_{n},a_{0}}(a)|\leq Cb_{n}^{-3}I(|a-a_{0}|\leq b_{n}),

and
|c_{0,h_{n},a_{0},2}|\leq C
.

Suppose first that
\tau>0
. For all sufficiently large
n
, the support of

u\mapsto h_{n}f_{A}(a_{0})\Gamma_{0,h_{n},b_{n},a_{0}}(a_{0}+h_{n}u)

is contained in a fixed compact interval. Direct substitution gives

\displaystyle h_{n}f_{A}(a_{0})\Gamma_{0,h_{n},b_{n},a_{0}}(a_{0}+h_{n}u)

\displaystyle=f_{A}(a_{0})e_{1}^{\top}D_{0,h_{n},a_{0},1}^{-1}(1,u)^{\top}K(u)

\displaystyle-f_{A}(a_{0})c_{0,h_{n},a_{0},2}\tau_{n}^{3}e_{3}^{\top}D_{0,b_{n},a_{0},2}^{-1}(1,\tau_{n}u,\tau_{n}^{2}u^{2})^{\top}K(\tau_{n}u).

By symmetry of
K
,

e_{1}^{\top}S_{2}^{-1}(1,u)^{\top}=1,\qquad e_{3}^{\top}S_{3}^{-1}(1,v,v^{2})^{\top}=\frac{v^{2}-c_{2}}{c_{4}-c_{2}^{2}}.

Lemma
6
therefore gives, uniformly on the fixed compact interval,

\displaystyle h_{n}f_{A}(a_{0})\Gamma_{0,h_{n},b_{n},a_{0}}(a_{0}+h_{n}u)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow K(u)-\tau^{3}c_{2}\frac{(\tau u)^{2}-c_{2}}{c_{4}-c_{2}^{2}}K(\tau u).

The change of variables
a=a_{0}+h_{n}u
and dominated convergence yield

E_{0}(V_{n,i}^{2})\longrightarrow\frac{V_{K,\tau}\sigma_{0}^{2}(a_{0})}{f_{A}(a_{0})}.

Suppose next that
\tau=0
. The local-linear component satisfies

\displaystyle h_{n}P_{0}\left[r_{0,h_{n},a_{0}}(A)^{2}\{\xi_{\infty}(O)-\theta_{0}(A)\}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\int\{e_{1}^{\top}D_{0,h_{n},a_{0},1}^{-1}(1,u)^{\top}K(u)\}^{2}\sigma_{0}^{2}(a_{0}+h_{n}u)f_{A}(a_{0}+h_{n}u)\,du

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\frac{c_{0}^{\ast}\sigma_{0}^{2}(a_{0})}{f_{A}(a_{0})}.

Since
h_{n}\leq b_{n}
for all sufficiently large
n
, the preceding bounds imply

\displaystyle P_{0}\left[|r_{0,h_{n},a_{0}}(A)s_{0,b_{n},a_{0}}(A)|\{\xi_{\infty}(O)-\theta_{0}(A)\}^{2}\right]\leq Cb_{n}^{-3},

\displaystyle P_{0}\left[s_{0,b_{n},a_{0}}(A)^{2}\{\xi_{\infty}(O)-\theta_{0}(A)\}^{2}\right]\leq Cb_{n}^{-5}.

Consequently,

\displaystyle h_{n}^{3}\left|P_{0}\left[r_{0,h_{n},a_{0}}(A)s_{0,b_{n},a_{0}}(A)\{\xi_{\infty}(O)-\theta_{0}(A)\}^{2}\right]\right|=O(\tau_{n}^{3}),

\displaystyle h_{n}^{5}P_{0}\left[s_{0,b_{n},a_{0}}(A)^{2}\{\xi_{\infty}(O)-\theta_{0}(A)\}^{2}\right]=O(\tau_{n}^{5}).

Therefore

E_{0}(V_{n,i}^{2})\longrightarrow\frac{c_{0}^{\ast}\sigma_{0}^{2}(a_{0})}{f_{A}(a_{0})}=\frac{V_{K,0}\sigma_{0}^{2}(a_{0})}{f_{A}(a_{0})}.

For every
\tau\in[0,\infty)
, the preceding envelope and moment bounds give

\displaystyle E_{0}|V_{n,1}|^{2+\delta_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq Ch_{n}^{(2+\delta_{2})/2}\left\{h_{n}^{-(2+\delta_{2})}h_{n}+h_{n}^{2(2+\delta_{2})}b_{n}^{-3(2+\delta_{2})}b_{n}\right\}

\displaystyle=Ch_{n}^{-\delta_{2}/2}\{1+\tau_{n}^{5+3\delta_{2}}\}

\displaystyle\leq Ch_{n}^{-\delta_{2}/2}.

If
\sigma_{0}^{2}(a_{0})>0
, then
E_{0}(V_{n,1}^{2})
converges to a positive finite constant and

\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}E_{0}|V_{n,i}|^{2+\delta_{2}}}{\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{n}E_{0}(V_{n,i}^{2})\right\}^{1+\delta_{2}/2}}=\frac{nE_{0}|V_{n,1}|^{2+\delta_{2}}}{\left\{nE_{0}(V_{n,1}^{2})\right\}^{1+\delta_{2}/2}}\leq C(nh_{n})^{-\delta_{2}/2}\to 0.

Lyapunov’s theorem therefore yields

\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}V_{n,i}\rightsquigarrow N\left(0,\frac{V_{K,\tau}\sigma_{0}^{2}(a_{0})}{f_{A}(a_{0})}\right).

If
\sigma_{0}^{2}(a_{0})=0
, then
E_{0}(V_{n,1}^{2})\to 0
, and Chebyshev’s inequality gives

\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}V_{n,i}\to_{p}0.

The two cases prove the claim.
∎

Recall

\phi_{\infty,a_{0}}(O)=\Gamma_{0,h_{n},b_{n},a_{0}}(A)\{\xi_{\infty}(O)-\theta_{0}(A)\}+\psi_{n,a_{0}}^{sm}(O).

Lemma 8
.

Fix
a_{0}
in the interior of the support of
A
. Under Assumptions
A
,
B
, and
D
–
F
, it holds that

P_{0}\psi_{n,a_{0}}^{sm}=0,

and

(nh_{n})^{1/2}\left|P_{n}\phi_{\infty,a_{0}}-P_{n}\left[\Gamma_{0,h_{n},b_{n},a_{0}}(A)\{\xi_{\infty}(O)-\theta_{0}(A)\}\right]\right|=(nh_{n})^{1/2}|P_{n}\psi_{n,a_{0}}^{sm}|\to_{p}0.

Proof.

For notational simplicity, write

\Gamma_{n}(a):=\Gamma_{0,h_{n},b_{n},a_{0}}(a),\qquad\gamma_{n}(a):=\gamma_{0,h_{n},b_{n},a_{0}}(a),

r_{n}(a):=r_{0,h_{n},a_{0}}(a),\qquad s_{n}(a):=s_{0,b_{n},a_{0}}(a),\qquad c_{n}:=c_{0,h_{n},a_{0},2},

\gamma_{n,h}(a):=\gamma_{0,h_{n},a_{0}}(a),\qquad\gamma_{n,b}^{\prime\prime}(a):=\gamma_{0,b_{n},a_{0}}^{\prime\prime}(a),\qquad\gamma_{n,h}^{c}(a):=\gamma_{0,h_{n},a_{0}}^{c}(a),

and

\theta_{n,b}^{\prime\prime}(a_{0}):=\theta_{0,b_{n}}^{\prime\prime}(a_{0}).

Then, note that

\Gamma_{n}(a)=r_{n}(a)-\frac{1}{2}h_{n}^{2}c_{n}s_{n}(a),

and

\gamma_{n}(a)=\gamma_{n,h}(a)-\frac{1}{2}h_{n}^{2}c_{n}\gamma_{n,b}^{\prime\prime}(a)+\frac{1}{2}h_{n}^{2}\theta_{n,b}^{\prime\prime}(a_{0})\gamma_{n,h}^{c}(a).

Since

\phi_{\infty,a_{0}}(O)=\Gamma_{n}(A)\{\xi_{\infty}(O)-\theta_{0}(A)\}+\psi_{n,a_{0}}^{sm}(O),

it only remains to prove that

(nh_{n})^{1/2}|P_{n}\psi_{n,a_{0}}^{sm}|\to_{p}0.

We first show that

P_{0}\psi_{n,a_{0}}^{sm}=0.

Indeed,

P_{0}\psi_{n,a_{0}}^{sm}=P_{0}\{\Gamma_{n}(A)\theta_{0}(A)\}-P_{0}\{\gamma_{n}(A)\}+\int\Gamma_{n}(\bar{a})E_{0}\{h_{\infty}(W,\bar{a},X)-m_{\infty}(\bar{a})\}\,dF_{0}(\bar{a}).

Since

m_{\infty}(\bar{a})=E_{0}\{h_{\infty}(W,\bar{a},X)\},

the integral term is zero. Therefore it suffices to show that

P_{0}\{\Gamma_{n}(A)\theta_{0}(A)\}=P_{0}\{\gamma_{n}(A)\}.

By the definition of
\Gamma_{n}
, we have

P_{0}\{\Gamma_{n}(A)\theta_{0}(A)\}=\theta_{0,h_{n}}^{LL}(a_{0})-\frac{1}{2}h_{n}^{2}c_{n}\theta_{n,b}^{\prime\prime}(a_{0}).

Next, we obtain

\displaystyle P_{0}\{\gamma_{n,h}(A)\}={}

\displaystyle e_{1}^{\top}D_{0,h_{n},a_{0},1}^{-1}P_{0}\{w_{h_{n},a_{0},1}(A)w_{h_{n},a_{0},1}(A)^{\top}K_{h_{n},a_{0}}(A)\}D_{0,h_{n},a_{0},1}^{-1}M_{0,h_{n},a_{0}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\theta_{0,h_{n}}^{LL}(a_{0}).

because

P_{0}\{w_{h_{n},a_{0},1}(A)w_{h_{n},a_{0},1}(A)^{\top}K_{h_{n},a_{0}}(A)\}=D_{0,h_{n},a_{0},1}.

Similarly, we get

P_{0}\{\gamma_{n,b}^{\prime\prime}(A)\}=2b_{n}^{-2}e_{3}^{\top}D_{0,b_{n},a_{0},2}^{-1}M_{0,b_{n},a_{0}}=\theta_{n,b}^{\prime\prime}(a_{0}).

Also, by the definition of
\gamma_{0,h_{n},a_{0}}^{c}
, we get
P_{0}\{\gamma_{n,h}^{c}(A)\}=0
.
Hence we have

\displaystyle P_{0}\{\gamma_{n}(A)\}

\displaystyle=P_{0}\{\gamma_{n,h}(A)\}-\frac{1}{2}h_{n}^{2}c_{n}P_{0}\{\gamma_{n,b}^{\prime\prime}(A)\}+\frac{1}{2}h_{n}^{2}\theta_{n,b}^{\prime\prime}(a_{0})P_{0}\{\gamma_{n,h}^{c}(A)\}

\displaystyle=\theta_{0,h_{n}}^{LL}(a_{0})-\frac{1}{2}h_{n}^{2}c_{n}\theta_{n,b}^{\prime\prime}(a_{0})

\displaystyle=P_{0}\{\Gamma_{n}(A)\theta_{0}(A)\}.

Therefore
P_{0}\psi_{n,a_{0}}^{sm}=0
.

Now write

\psi_{n,a_{0}}^{sm}(O)=U_{n}(O)+V_{n}(O),

where

U_{n}(O):=\Gamma_{n}(A)\theta_{0}(A)-\gamma_{n}(A)

and

V_{n}(O):=\int\Gamma_{n}(\bar{a})\{h_{\infty}(W,\bar{a},X)-m_{\infty}(\bar{a})\}\,dF_{0}(\bar{a}).

Then

P_{0}(\psi_{n,a_{0}}^{sm})^{2}\leq 2P_{0}U_{n}^{2}+2P_{0}V_{n}^{2}.

We first bound
P_{0}V_{n}^{2}
. By boundedness of
h_{\infty}
on a neighbourhood of

a_{0}
, there exists a finite constant
C_{h}
such that

|h_{\infty}(W,\bar{a},X)-m_{\infty}(\bar{a})|\leq 2C_{h}

for all
\bar{a}
in the support of
\Gamma_{n}
. Therefore

|V_{n}(O)|\leq 2C_{h}\int|\Gamma_{n}(\bar{a})|\,dF_{0}(\bar{a}).

We next show that

\int|\Gamma_{n}(\bar{a})|\,dF_{0}(\bar{a})\leq C

for a constant
C
not depending on
n
. By Lemma
6
, we have

\|D_{0,h_{n},a_{0},1}^{-1}\|_{\infty}\leq C,\qquad\|D_{0,b_{n},a_{0},2}^{-1}\|_{\infty}\leq C,\qquad|c_{n}|\leq C

for all sufficiently large
n
. Since
K
is supported on
[-1,1]
, the coordinates of

w_{h_{n},a_{0},1}(a)
are uniformly bounded on the support of
K_{h_{n},a_{0}}
, and the
coordinates of
w_{b_{n},a_{0},2}(a)
are uniformly bounded on the support of

K_{b_{n},a_{0}}
. Hence there exists
C<\infty
such that

|r_{n}(a)|\leq Ch_{n}^{-1}I(|a-a_{0}|\leq h_{n}),

and

|s_{n}(a)|\leq Cb_{n}^{-3}I(|a-a_{0}|\leq b_{n}).

Therefore

|\Gamma_{n}(a)|\leq Ch_{n}^{-1}I(|a-a_{0}|\leq h_{n})+Ch_{n}^{2}b_{n}^{-3}I(|a-a_{0}|\leq b_{n}).

Since
f_{A}
is bounded on a neighborhood of
a_{0}
by Assumption
E1
,

\int|\Gamma_{n}(a)|\,dF_{0}(a)\leq Ch_{n}^{-1}\int_{|a-a_{0}|\leq h_{n}}f_{A}(a)\,da+Ch_{n}^{2}b_{n}^{-3}\int_{|a-a_{0}|\leq b_{n}}f_{A}(a)\,da

\leq C+Ch_{n}^{2}b_{n}^{-2}=C+C(h_{n}/b_{n})^{2}\leq C,

because
h_{n}/b_{n}\to\tau\in[0,\infty)
by Assumption
D2
. Consequently,
we get
|V_{n}(O)|\leq C
, which further yields

P_{0}V_{n}^{2}\leq C
.

We next bound
P_{0}U_{n}^{2}
. Write

U_{n}(a)=U_{n,1}(a)+U_{n,2}(a)+U_{n,3}(a),

where

U_{n,1}(a):=r_{n}(a)\{\theta_{0}(a)-w_{h_{n},a_{0},1}(a)^{\top}\beta_{n,h}\},

U_{n,2}(a):=-\frac{1}{2}h_{n}^{2}c_{n}s_{n}(a)\{\theta_{0}(a)-w_{b_{n},a_{0},2}(a)^{\top}\beta_{n,b}\},

U_{n,3}(a):=-\frac{1}{2}h_{n}^{2}\theta_{n,b}^{\prime\prime}(a_{0})\gamma_{n,h}^{c}(a),

and

\beta_{n,h}:=D_{0,h_{n},a_{0},1}^{-1}M_{0,h_{n},a_{0}},\qquad\beta_{n,b}:=D_{0,b_{n},a_{0},2}^{-1}M_{0,b_{n},a_{0}}.

We begin with
U_{n,1}
. Define

\beta_{n,h}^{\star}:=\begin{pmatrix}\theta_{0}(a_{0})\\
h_{n}\theta_{0}^{\prime}(a_{0})\end{pmatrix}.

Then

\beta_{n,h}-\beta_{n,h}^{\star}=D_{0,h_{n},a_{0},1}^{-1}P_{0}\left[w_{h_{n},a_{0},1}(A)K_{h_{n},a_{0}}(A)\{\theta_{0}(A)-w_{h_{n},a_{0},1}(A)^{\top}\beta_{n,h}^{\star}\}\right].

For
|A-a_{0}|\leq h_{n}
, Taylor’s expansion yields

\theta_{0}(A)-w_{h_{n},a_{0},1}(A)^{\top}\beta_{n,h}^{\star}=\theta_{0}(A)-\theta_{0}(a_{0})-\theta_{0}^{\prime}(a_{0})(A-a_{0})=\frac{1}{2}(A-a_{0})^{2}\theta_{0}^{\prime\prime}(\widetilde{A})

for some
\widetilde{A}
between
A
and
a_{0}
. Since
\theta_{0}^{\prime\prime}
is bounded on

B_{\delta_{1}}(a_{0})
by Assumption
E1
, there exists
C<\infty
such that

|\theta_{0}(A)-w_{h_{n},a_{0},1}(A)^{\top}\beta_{n,h}^{\star}|\leq Ch_{n}^{2}

whenever
|A-a_{0}|\leq h_{n}
. Therefore

\|\beta_{n,h}-\beta_{n,h}^{\star}\|_{1}\leq Ch_{n}^{2}.

Now if
|a-a_{0}|\leq h_{n}
, then the coordinates of
w_{h_{n},a_{0},1}(a)
are uniformly
bounded, so

\displaystyle|\theta_{0}(a)-w_{h_{n},a_{0},1}(a)^{\top}\beta_{n,h}|{}

\displaystyle\leq|\theta_{0}(a)-w_{h_{n},a_{0},1}(a)^{\top}\beta_{n,h}^{\star}|+|w_{h_{n},a_{0},1}(a)^{\top}(\beta_{n,h}-\beta_{n,h}^{\star})|

\displaystyle\leq Ch_{n}^{2}.

Combining this with

|r_{n}(a)|\leq Ch_{n}^{-1}I(|a-a_{0}|\leq h_{n}),

we obtain

|U_{n,1}(a)|\leq Ch_{n}I(|a-a_{0}|\leq h_{n}).

Hence

P_{0}U_{n,1}^{2}\leq Ch_{n}^{2}P_{0}(|A-a_{0}|\leq h_{n})\leq Ch_{n}^{2}\int_{|a-a_{0}|\leq h_{n}}f_{A}(a)\,da=O(h_{n}^{3}).

For
U_{n,2}
, define

\beta_{n,b}^{\star}:=\begin{pmatrix}\theta_{0}(a_{0})\\
b_{n}\theta_{0}^{\prime}(a_{0})\\
\frac{1}{2}b_{n}^{2}\theta_{0}^{\prime\prime}(a_{0})\end{pmatrix}.

Then

\beta_{n,b}-\beta_{n,b}^{\star}=D_{0,b_{n},a_{0},2}^{-1}P_{0}\left[w_{b_{n},a_{0},2}(A)K_{b_{n},a_{0}}(A)\{\theta_{0}(A)-w_{b_{n},a_{0},2}(A)^{\top}\beta_{n,b}^{\star}\}\right].

Since
\theta_{0}
is twice continuously differentiable on
B_{\delta_{1}}(a_{0})
,
second-order Taylor expansion yields

\theta_{0}(A)-w_{b_{n},a_{0},2}(A)^{\top}\beta_{n,b}^{\star}=\frac{1}{2}(A-a_{0})^{2}\{\theta_{0}^{\prime\prime}(\widetilde{A})-\theta_{0}^{\prime\prime}(a_{0})\}

for some
\widetilde{A}
between
A
and
a_{0}
. Because
\theta_{0}^{\prime\prime}
is continuous on

B_{\delta_{1}}(a_{0})
, it is bounded there, and so

|\theta_{0}(A)-w_{b_{n},a_{0},2}(A)^{\top}\beta_{n,b}^{\star}|\leq Cb_{n}^{2}

for
|A-a_{0}|\leq b_{n}
. Hence

\|\beta_{n,b}-\beta_{n,b}^{\star}\|_{1}\leq Cb_{n}^{2}.

If
|a-a_{0}|\leq b_{n}
, the coordinates of
w_{b_{n},a_{0},2}(a)
are uniformly bounded,
and therefore

|\theta_{0}(a)-w_{b_{n},a_{0},2}(a)^{\top}\beta_{n,b}|\leq Cb_{n}^{2}.

Using

|s_{n}(a)|\leq Cb_{n}^{-3}I(|a-a_{0}|\leq b_{n})

and
|c_{n}|\leq C
, we get

|U_{n,2}(a)|\leq Ch_{n}^{2}b_{n}^{-1}I(|a-a_{0}|\leq b_{n}).

Since
h_{n}/b_{n}=O(1)
by Assumption
D2
, this implies

|U_{n,2}(a)|\leq Ch_{n}I(|a-a_{0}|\leq b_{n}).

Consequently,

P_{0}U_{n,2}^{2}\leq Ch_{n}^{2}P_{0}(|A-a_{0}|\leq b_{n})=O(h_{n}^{2}b_{n}).

For
U_{n,3}
, we first show that
\theta_{n,b}^{\prime\prime}(a_{0})
is bounded.
Since

\beta_{n,b}=\beta_{n,b}^{\star}+O(b_{n}^{2}),

its third coordinate satisfies

e_{3}^{\top}\beta_{n,b}=\frac{1}{2}b_{n}^{2}\theta_{0}^{\prime\prime}(a_{0})+O(b_{n}^{2})=O(b_{n}^{2}).

Therefore

\theta_{n,b}^{\prime\prime}(a_{0})=2b_{n}^{-2}e_{3}^{\top}\beta_{n,b}=O(1).

Next, by the definition of
\gamma_{n,h}^{c}
, we get

\displaystyle\gamma_{n,h}^{c}(a)

\displaystyle=e_{1}^{\top}D_{0,h_{n},a_{0},1}^{-1}\Bigl[\widetilde{w}_{h_{n},a_{0},1}(a)-w_{h_{n},a_{0},1}(a)w_{h_{n},a_{0},1}(a)^{\top}D_{0,h_{n},a_{0},1}^{-1}P_{0}\{\widetilde{w}_{h_{n},a_{0},1}(A)K_{h_{n},a_{0}}(A)\}\Bigr]K_{h_{n},a_{0}}(a).

The bracketed term is uniformly bounded on the support of
K_{h_{n},a_{0}}
, and

D_{0,h_{n},a_{0},1}^{-1}
is uniformly bounded by Lemma
6
. Hence

|\gamma_{n,h}^{c}(a)|\leq Ch_{n}^{-1}I(|a-a_{0}|\leq h_{n}).

Since
\theta_{n,b}^{\prime\prime}(a_{0})=O(1)
, it follows that

|U_{n,3}(a)|\leq Ch_{n}I(|a-a_{0}|\leq h_{n}),

and therefore

P_{0}U_{n,3}^{2}=O(h_{n}^{3}).

Combining the three bounds,

P_{0}U_{n}^{2}\leq 3P_{0}U_{n,1}^{2}+3P_{0}U_{n,2}^{2}+3P_{0}U_{n,3}^{2}=O(h_{n}^{3})+O(h_{n}^{2}b_{n})+O(h_{n}^{3}).

Hence

P_{0}(\psi_{n,a_{0}}^{sm})^{2}\leq 2P_{0}U_{n}^{2}+2P_{0}V_{n}^{2}\leq C

for all sufficiently large
n
.

Since
P_{0}\psi_{n,a_{0}}^{sm}=0
,

E_{0}\left[\left\{(nh_{n})^{1/2}P_{n}\psi_{n,a_{0}}^{sm}\right\}^{2}\right]=nh_{n}E_{0}\left[\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\psi_{n,a_{0}}^{sm}(O_{i})\right)^{2}\right]=h_{n}P_{0}(\psi_{n,a_{0}}^{sm})^{2}\to 0.

Thus

(nh_{n})^{1/2}P_{n}\psi_{n,a_{0}}^{sm}\to 0

in
L_{2}(P_{0})
and hence in probability, which proves the claim.
∎

Lemma 9
.

Fix distinct points
a_{1},\ldots,a_{m}
in the interior of the support of
A
.
Assume Assumptions
A
,
B
, and
D
–
F
hold at each
a_{k}
for the same deterministic pair
(q_{\infty},h_{\infty})
. Then

(nh_{n})^{1/2}\begin{pmatrix}P_{n}\left[\Gamma_{0,h_{n},b_{n},a_{1}}(A)\{\xi_{\infty}(O)-\theta_{0}(A)\}\right]\\
\vdots\\
P_{n}\left[\Gamma_{0,h_{n},b_{n},a_{m}}(A)\{\xi_{\infty}(O)-\theta_{0}(A)\}\right]\end{pmatrix}

converges in distribution to a mean-zero multivariate normal vector with diagonal
covariance matrix

\operatorname{diag}\left(\frac{V_{K,\tau}\sigma_{0}^{2}(a_{1})}{f_{A}(a_{1})},\ldots,\frac{V_{K,\tau}\sigma_{0}^{2}(a_{m})}{f_{A}(a_{m})}\right).

Proof.

We use the Cramér–Wold device. Let
t_{1},\ldots,t_{m}\in\mathbb{R}
be fixed and
define

X_{n,i}(t):=h_{n}^{1/2}\sum_{k=1}^{m}t_{k}\Gamma_{0,h_{n},b_{n},a_{k}}(A_{i})\{\xi_{\infty}(O_{i})-\theta_{0}(A_{i})\}.

Then

(nh_{n})^{1/2}\sum_{k=1}^{m}t_{k}P_{n}\left[\Gamma_{0,h_{n},b_{n},a_{k}}(A)\{\xi_{\infty}(O)-\theta_{0}(A)\}\right]=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{n,i}(t).

Let

\Delta:=\frac{1}{2}\min_{1\leq r<s\leq m}|a_{r}-a_{s}|>0.

Since
h_{n}\to 0
and
b_{n}\to 0
, for all sufficiently large
n
we have

\max\{h_{n},b_{n}\}<\Delta
.
Because
K
is supported on
[-1,1]
, the supports of

a\mapsto\Gamma_{0,h_{n},b_{n},a_{r}}(a)\quad\text{and}\quad a\mapsto\Gamma_{0,h_{n},b_{n},a_{s}}(a)

are disjoint whenever
r\neq s
and
n
is large enough.
Hence

E_{0}\{X_{n,i}(t)\}=0

and

\begin{split}E_{0}\{X_{n,i}(t)^{2}\}&=h_{n}E_{0}\left[\left\{\sum_{k=1}^{m}t_{k}\Gamma_{0,h_{n},b_{n},a_{k}}(A)\{\xi_{\infty}(O)-\theta_{0}(A)\}\right\}^{2}\right]\\
&=\sum_{k=1}^{m}t_{k}^{2}h_{n}E_{0}\left[\Gamma_{0,h_{n},b_{n},a_{k}}(A)^{2}\{\xi_{\infty}(O)-\theta_{0}(A)\}^{2}\right].\end{split}

By the variance calculation in the proof of Lemma
7
,

E_{0}\{X_{n,i}(t)^{2}\}\to\sum_{k=1}^{m}t_{k}^{2}\frac{V_{K,\tau}\sigma_{0}^{2}(a_{k})}{f_{A}(a_{k})}.

If the limit in the preceding display is zero, then Chebyshev’s inequality gives

\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{n,i}(t)\to_{p}0.

Suppose that the limit is positive.
The Lyapunov condition is verified exactly as in the proof of Lemma
7
. Indeed,
using disjoint supports,

|X_{n,1}(t)|^{2+\delta_{2}}\leq C\sum_{k=1}^{m}|t_{k}|^{2+\delta_{2}}h_{n}^{(2+\delta_{2})/2}|\Gamma_{0,h_{n},b_{n},a_{k}}(A)|^{2+\delta_{2}}|\xi_{\infty}(O)-\theta_{0}(A)|^{2+\delta_{2}},

and the same calculation as before yields

E_{0}|X_{n,1}(t)|^{2+\delta_{2}}\leq Ch_{n}^{-\delta_{2}/2}.

Therefore

\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}E_{0}|X_{n,i}(t)|^{2+\delta_{2}}}{\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{n}E_{0}(X_{n,i}(t)^{2})\right\}^{1+\delta_{2}/2}}\leq C(nh_{n})^{-\delta_{2}/2}\to 0.

Lyapunov’s theorem gives the scalar normal limit. The zero-variance case and the positive-variance case apply to every
(t_{1},\ldots,t_{m})
. The Cramér–Wold device completes the proof.
∎

C.3
Asymptotic confidence band

Lemma 10
.

Let
h=h_{n}
and
b=b_{n}
. Under Assumptions
A
,
B
,

D
, and
G
,

\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\sigma_{\infty,h,b}^{2}(a_{0})-\frac{V_{K,\tau}\sigma_{0}^{2}(a_{0})}{f_{A}(a_{0})}\right|\longrightarrow 0.

Consequently, there exist constants
0<c<C<\infty
such that, for all
sufficiently large
n
,

c\leq\inf_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\sigma_{\infty,h,b}^{2}(a_{0})\leq\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\sigma_{\infty,h,b}^{2}(a_{0})\leq C.

Proof.

The identity

\phi_{\infty,h,b,a_{0}}=\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(A)\{\xi_{\infty}-\theta_{0}(A)\}+\psi_{n,a_{0}}^{sm}

holds. The local-polynomial normal equations, Taylor’s expansion, and
Lemma
6
give

\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}P_{0}\left[\{\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(A)\theta_{0}(A)-\gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(A)\}^{2}\right]=O(h)

and

\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}P_{0}\left[\left\{\int\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(\bar{a})\{h_{\infty}(W,\bar{a},X)-m_{\infty}(\bar{a})\}\,dF_{0}(\bar{a})\right\}^{2}\right]=O(1).

These are the bounds in the proof of Lemma 5 of

Takatsu and Westling (2025)
, with
\mu_{\infty}(a,W)
replaced by

h_{\infty}(W,a,X)
. Assumptions
G3
(i)
and
G5
make the bounds uniform over

a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}
. Hence

\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}P_{0}\{(\psi_{n,a_{0}}^{sm})^{2}\}=O(1).

The kernel support, Lemma
6
, and

\tau_{n}=h/b\to\tau\in(0,\infty)
also give

\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}hP_{0}\left[\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(A)^{2}\{\xi_{\infty}-\theta_{0}(A)\}^{2}\right]=O(1).

Therefore, by the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality,

\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\sigma_{\infty,h,b}^{2}(a_{0})-h\int\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(a)^{2}\sigma_{0}^{2}(a)f_{A}(a)\,da\right|=O(h^{1/2}).

Lemma
6
yields

h\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(a_{0}+hu)=\frac{1}{f_{A}(a_{0})}\left\{K(u)-\tau_{n}^{3}c_{2}\frac{(\tau_{n}u)^{2}-c_{2}}{c_{4}-c_{2}^{2}}K(\tau_{n}u)\right\}+r_{h}(a_{0},u),

where, for some fixed
C<\infty
,

\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\sup_{u\in[-C,C]}|r_{h}(a_{0},u)|\longrightarrow 0,

and the preceding functions vanish outside
[-C,C]
for all sufficiently
large
n
. The change of variables
a=a_{0}+hu
gives

\displaystyle h\int\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(a)^{2}\sigma_{0}^{2}(a)f_{A}(a)\,da

\displaystyle=\int\left[\frac{1}{f_{A}(a_{0})}\left\{K(u)-\tau_{n}^{3}c_{2}\frac{(\tau_{n}u)^{2}-c_{2}}{c_{4}-c_{2}^{2}}K(\tau_{n}u)\right\}+r_{h}(a_{0},u)\right]^{2}\sigma_{0}^{2}(a_{0}+hu)f_{A}(a_{0}+hu)\,du.

Continuity on the compact set
\mathcal{A}_{\delta_{3}}
implies

\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\sup_{u\in[-C,C]}\left|\sigma_{0}^{2}(a_{0}+hu)f_{A}(a_{0}+hu)-\sigma_{0}^{2}(a_{0})f_{A}(a_{0})\right|\longrightarrow 0.

Dominated convergence and
\tau_{n}\to\tau\in(0,\infty)
prove the asserted
uniform convergence.

Jensen’s inequality gives
c_{4}>c_{2}^{2}
. If
\tau\neq 1
, the essential supports
of
K(u)
and
K(\tau u)
differ. If
\tau=1
, a zero value of
V_{K,1}

would imply
u^{2}=c_{4}/c_{2}
for
K(u)\,du
-almost every
u
, contrary to

c_{4}>c_{2}^{2}
. Thus
V_{K,\tau}\in(0,\infty)
. Assumption

G5
(ii), (iv) gives the asserted lower and upper
bounds.
∎

Lemma 11
.

Let
h=h_{n}
and
b=b_{n}
. Under Assumptions
A
,
B
,

D
, and
G
, for every integer
k\geq 1
,

\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}P_{0}|h\phi_{\infty,h,b,a_{0}}|^{k}\lesssim h.

Equivalently,

\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}P_{0}|\phi_{\infty,h,b,a_{0}}|^{k}\lesssim h^{-(k-1)}.

In particular,

\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}P_{0}\left|\frac{h^{1/2}\phi_{\infty,h,b,a_{0}}}{\sigma_{\infty,h,b}(a_{0})}\right|^{3}\lesssim h^{-1/2}.

Proof.

Assumptions
G3
(i)
and
G5
(iii) imply

\|\xi_{\infty}\|_{\infty}<\infty
. Lemma
6
and

\tau_{n}=h/b\to\tau\in(0,\infty)
give, uniformly in

a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}
,

|\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(a)|\leq C\left[h^{-1}I\{|a-a_{0}|\leq h\}+h^{2}b^{-3}I\{|a-a_{0}|\leq b\}\right].

Consequently,

\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}P_{0}|h\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(A)\xi_{\infty}|^{k}\lesssim h.

The local-polynomial coefficient vectors in
\gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}
are uniformly
bounded by the normal equations and Taylor’s expansion. Moreover,

\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}|\theta_{0,b}^{\prime\prime}(a_{0})|=O(1)
. Hence

|\gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(a)|\leq C\left[h^{-1}I\{|a-a_{0}|\leq h\}+h^{2}b^{-3}I\{|a-a_{0}|\leq b\}+hI\{|a-a_{0}|\leq h\}\right],

and therefore

\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}P_{0}|h\gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(A)|^{k}\lesssim h.

Finally,

\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\int|\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(\bar{a})|\,dF_{0}(\bar{a})\leq C,

so the boundedness of
h_{\infty}
implies

\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}P_{0}\left|h\int\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(\bar{a})\{h_{\infty}(W,\bar{a},X)-m_{\infty}(\bar{a})\}\,dF_{0}(\bar{a})\right|^{k}\lesssim h^{k}.

Minkowski’s inequality proves the first assertion. This is also the argument
of Lemma 23 of
Takatsu and Westling (2025)
, with

\mu_{\infty}(a,W)
replaced by
h_{\infty}(W,a,X)
. The last assertion follows
from Lemma
10
.
∎

Lemma 12
.

Let
h=h_{n}
and
b=b_{n}
. Under Assumptions
A
,
B
,

D
, and
G
, the mean-zero Gaussian process

Z_{\infty,h,b}
is tight in
\ell^{\infty}(\mathcal{A}_{0})
and

E_{0}\left\{\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}|Z_{\infty,h,b}(a_{0})|\right\}\leq C\{\log(h^{-1})\}^{1/2}.

For every
\delta>0
,

\displaystyle E_{0}\left[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}u,v\in\mathcal{A}_{0}\\
|u-v|<\delta\end{subarray}}|Z_{\infty,h,b}(u)-Z_{\infty,h,b}(v)|\right]\leq Ch^{-1/2}(\delta\wedge h)^{1/2}\left[1+\log\left\{1+\frac{\operatorname{diam}(\mathcal{A}_{0})}{\delta\wedge h}\right\}\right]^{1/2}.

Proof.

For
u,v\in\mathcal{A}_{0}
, the kernel support, the Lipschitz continuity of

K
and
f_{A}
, the inverse-difference identity, and
Lemma
6
give

\|\Gamma_{0,h,b,u}-\Gamma_{0,h,b,v}\|_{P_{0},2}\leq C\left\{h^{-1/2}\wedge h^{-3/2}|u-v|\right\}.

The same calculations applied to the three terms defining

\gamma_{0,h,b,u}
give

\|\gamma_{0,h,b,u}-\gamma_{0,h,b,v}\|_{P_{0},2}\leq C\left\{h^{-1/2}\wedge h^{-3/2}|u-v|\right\}.

Indeed, on the union of the two kernel supports,

\sup_{a}|\Gamma_{0,h,b,u}(a)-\Gamma_{0,h,b,v}(a)|+\sup_{a}|\gamma_{0,h,b,u}(a)-\gamma_{0,h,b,v}(a)|\leq Ch^{-2}|u-v|,

whereas the
L_{2}(P_{0})
norm of each summand at one index is
O(h^{-1/2})
.
Moreover, boundedness of
h_{\infty}
gives

\displaystyle\left\|\int\{\Gamma_{0,h,b,u}(\bar{a})-\Gamma_{0,h,b,v}(\bar{a})\}\{h_{\infty}(W,\bar{a},X)-m_{\infty}(\bar{a})\}\,dF_{0}(\bar{a})\right\|_{P_{0},2}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq C\int|\Gamma_{0,h,b,u}(\bar{a})-\Gamma_{0,h,b,v}(\bar{a})|\,dF_{0}(\bar{a})

\displaystyle\qquad\leq C\{1\wedge h^{-1}|u-v|\}.

Since
\xi_{\infty}
is bounded, the definition of the full influence function
therefore yields

\|\phi_{\infty,h,b,u}-\phi_{\infty,h,b,v}\|_{P_{0},2}\leq C\left\{h^{-1/2}\wedge h^{-3/2}|u-v|\right\}.

These are the full-influence function increment bounds in the proof of
Lemma 24 of
Takatsu and Westling (2025)
; the integral bound above is the
proximal analogue obtained by replacing
\mu_{\infty}(a,W)
with

h_{\infty}(W,a,X)
.

Let
\rho_{\infty,h,b}
be the canonical semimetric of

Z_{\infty,h,b}
. Lemma
10
and the reverse triangle
inequality in
L_{2}(P_{0})
give

|\sigma_{\infty,h,b}(u)-\sigma_{\infty,h,b}(v)|\leq h^{1/2}\|\phi_{\infty,h,b,u}-\phi_{\infty,h,b,v}\|_{P_{0},2}.

Consequently,

\displaystyle\rho_{\infty,h,b}(u,v)

\displaystyle=\left\|\frac{h^{1/2}\phi_{\infty,h,b,u}}{\sigma_{\infty,h,b}(u)}-\frac{h^{1/2}\phi_{\infty,h,b,v}}{\sigma_{\infty,h,b}(v)}\right\|_{P_{0},2}

\displaystyle\leq Ch^{1/2}\|\phi_{\infty,h,b,u}-\phi_{\infty,h,b,v}\|_{P_{0},2}

and hence

\rho_{\infty,h,b}(u,v)\leq C\left[1\wedge\{h^{-1/2}|u-v|^{1/2}\}\right].

It follows that

N(\varepsilon,\mathcal{A}_{0},\rho_{\infty,h,b})\leq C\left\{1+\frac{\operatorname{diam}(\mathcal{A}_{0})}{h\varepsilon^{2}}\right\},\qquad 0<\varepsilon\leq 1.

The entropy integral is finite. Corollary 2.2.8 of

van der Vaart and Wellner (2023)
gives

E_{0}\left\{\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}|Z_{\infty,h,b}(a_{0})|\right\}\leq C\{\log(h^{-1})\}^{1/2}

and the asserted modulus bound. The same entropy integral implies almost
sure uniform continuity in the canonical semimetric. Total boundedness of

(\mathcal{A}_{0},\rho_{\infty,h,b})
proves tightness.
∎

Lemma 13
.

Let
h=h_{n}
and
b=b_{n}
. Under Assumptions
A
,
B
,

D
, and
G
, define

\eta_{h,b,a_{0}}:=\frac{h^{1/2}\phi_{\infty,h,b,a_{0}}}{\sigma_{\infty,h,b}(a_{0})},\qquad\mathcal{H}_{h,b}:=\{\eta_{h,b,a_{0}}:a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}\},

and
\mathcal{F}_{h,b}:=\mathcal{H}_{h,b}\cup(-\mathcal{H}_{h,b})
.
The class
\mathcal{F}_{h,b}
is pointwise measurable and VC–type, with
characteristics independent of
n
, and has an envelope
F_{h,b}
satisfying

\|F_{h,b}\|_{P_{0},3}\leq Ch^{-1/2},\qquad\|F_{h,b}\|_{P_{0},4}\leq Ch^{-1/2}.

There exists a random variable

\widetilde{Z}_{n,h,b}\overset{d}{=}\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}|Z_{\infty,h,b}(a_{0})|

such that

\left|\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}|G_{n}\eta_{h,b,a_{0}}|-\widetilde{Z}_{n,h,b}\right|=O_{p}(r_{n}),

where

r_{n}=(nh)^{-1/6}\log n+(nh)^{-1/4}(\log n)^{5/4}+(nh)^{-1/2}(\log n)^{3/2}.

Moreover,

\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|P_{0}\left\{\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}|G_{n}\eta_{h,b,a_{0}}|\leq t\right\}-P_{0}\left\{\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}|Z_{\infty,h,b}(a_{0})|\leq t\right\}\right|\longrightarrow 0.

Proof.

For every fixed observation
o
, the maps in
a_{0}
defining

D_{0,h,a_{0},1}
,
D_{0,b,a_{0},2}
,
\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}
, and

\gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}
are continuous. Dominated convergence gives continuity
of the integral term in
\phi_{\infty,h,b,a_{0}}(o)
.
Lemma
10
and the increment bound in
Lemma
12
give continuity of

a_{0}\mapsto\sigma_{\infty,h,b}(a_{0})
. Thus

a_{0}\mapsto\eta_{h,b,a_{0}}(o)
is continuous. Compactness of
\mathcal{A}_{0}

gives a countable dense subset and a countable
pointwise-dense subclass of
\mathcal{F}_{h,b}
.

Assumption
D1
and Lemma 11 of

Takatsu and Westling (2025)
imply that the translated kernel-polynomial
classes entering
\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}
and
\gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}
are VC–type.
Multiplication by the fixed bounded function
\xi_{\infty}
preserves VC–type.

Lemma 5.2 of
van der Vaart and van der Laan (2006)
applied to

\left\{(\bar{a},w,x)\mapsto\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(\bar{a})\{h_{\infty}(w,\bar{a},x)-m_{\infty}(\bar{a})\}:a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}\right\}

shows that the corresponding classes obtained by integration with respect
to
F_{0}
are VC–type. Sums, products, and multiplication by the constants

\sigma_{\infty,h,b}(a_{0})^{-1}
preserve this property. This is the
full-influence function argument in Corollary 5 of

Takatsu and Westling (2025)
. Hence
\mathcal{F}_{h,b}
is VC–type with
characteristics independent of
n
.

The kernel support, the uniform matrix bounds, boundedness of
\xi_{\infty}

and
h_{\infty}
, and Lemma
10
give the deterministic
envelope

F_{h,b}=Ch^{-1/2}.

Thus the asserted
L_{3}(P_{0})
and
L_{4}(P_{0})
envelope bounds hold. Moreover,

P_{0}\eta_{h,b,a_{0}}^{2}=1

and Lemma
11
gives, for
k=3,4
,

\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}P_{0}|\eta_{h,b,a_{0}}|^{k}\leq Ch^{1-k/2}.

Lemma
12
proves that

\mathcal{F}_{h,b}
is
P_{0}
-pre-Gaussian.

Corollary 2.2 of
Chernozhukov et al. (2014)
, applied with

\gamma_{n}=(\log n)^{-1}
, now gives the stated coupling and the displayed
value of
r_{n}
. Assumption
G1
implies

r_{n}\{\log(h^{-1})\}^{1/2}\longrightarrow 0.

Lemma 2.4 of
Chernozhukov et al. (2014)
, together with
Lemma
12
, gives the asserted convergence in
Kolmogorov distance. This is the argument of Lemma 25 of

Takatsu and Westling (2025)
.
∎

Corollary 1
.

Under the conditions of the preceding lemma,

\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|P_{0}\left(\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\frac{(nh)^{1/2}P_{n}\phi_{\infty,h,b,a_{0}}}{\sigma_{\infty,h,b}(a_{0})}\right|\leq t\right)-P_{0}\left(\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}|Z_{\infty,h,b}(a_{0})|\leq t\right)\right|\to 0.

Proof.

Assumptions
A
,
B
, and

G3
(i) and
Theorem
1
give

E_{0}\{\xi_{\infty}(O)-\theta_{0}(A)\mid A=a\}=0

for
f_{A}
-almost every
a\in\mathcal{A}_{\delta_{3}}
. For all
sufficiently large
n
, the support of

\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}
is contained in
\mathcal{A}_{\delta_{3}}
,
uniformly over
a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}
. The calculation of

P_{0}\psi_{n,a_{0}}^{sm}
in the proof of
Lemma
8
therefore gives

P_{0}\phi_{\infty,h,b,a_{0}}=0.

For every
a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}
,

G_{n}\eta_{h,b,a_{0}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\{\eta_{h,b,a_{0}}(O_{i})-P_{0}\eta_{h,b,a_{0}}\right\}=\frac{(nh)^{1/2}P_{n}\phi_{\infty,h,b,a_{0}}}{\sigma_{\infty,h,b}(a_{0})},

because
P_{0}\eta_{h,b,a_{0}}=0
. The claim follows directly.
∎

Lemma 14
.

Under the conditions of Lemma
12
, let

\mathcal{A}_{n}\subset\mathcal{A}_{0}
be a finite mesh and define

\omega_{n}:=\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\inf_{a\in\mathcal{A}_{n}}|a_{0}-a|.

If
\omega_{n}=o(h^{p})
for some
p>1
, then

\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|P_{0}\left\{\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}|Z_{\infty,h,b}(a_{0})|\leq t\right\}-P_{0}\left\{\max_{a\in\mathcal{A}_{n}}|Z_{\infty,h,b}(a)|\leq t\right\}\right|\longrightarrow 0.

Proof.

If
\omega_{n}=0
, the two random variables in the statement are equal.
It therefore suffices to consider indices for which
\omega_{n}>0
.
The mesh definition gives

\left|\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}|Z_{\infty,h,b}(a_{0})|-\max_{a\in\mathcal{A}_{n}}|Z_{\infty,h,b}(a)|\right|\leq\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}u,v\in\mathcal{A}_{0}\\
|u-v|\leq\omega_{n}\end{subarray}}|Z_{\infty,h,b}(u)-Z_{\infty,h,b}(v)|.

For every
\varepsilon>0
, the Kolmogorov distance in the statement is
bounded by

\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}P_{0}\left\{\left|\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}|Z_{\infty,h,b}(a_{0})|-t\right|\leq\varepsilon\{\log(h^{-1})\}^{-1/2}\right\}

plus

P_{0}\left\{\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}u,v\in\mathcal{A}_{0}\\
|u-v|\leq\omega_{n}\end{subarray}}|Z_{\infty,h,b}(u)-Z_{\infty,h,b}(v)|>\varepsilon\{\log(h^{-1})\}^{-1/2}\right\}.

Lemma A.1 of
Chernozhukov et al. (2014)
, applied
to the symmetrized unit-variance class, and
Lemma
12
imply that the first term is at
most
C\varepsilon+o(1)
; this is Lemma 26 of

Takatsu and Westling (2025)
. Markov’s inequality and
Lemma
12
bound the second term by

\frac{C}{\varepsilon}\{\log(h^{-1})\}^{1/2}\left(\frac{\omega_{n}}{h}\right)^{1/2}\left[1+\log\left\{1+\frac{\operatorname{diam}(\mathcal{A}_{0})}{\omega_{n}}\right\}\right]^{1/2}

for all sufficiently large
n
, since
\omega_{n}/h\to 0
. The square of the
last display, apart from its fixed multiplicative constant, is bounded by

\frac{\omega_{n}}{h}\log(h^{-1})\left[1+\log\left\{1+\frac{\operatorname{diam}(\mathcal{A}_{0})}{\omega_{n}}\right\}\right],

which converges to zero when
\omega_{n}=o(h^{p})
for some
p>1
. Indeed,

\omega_{n}/h\leq h^{(p-1)/2}
eventually, and monotonicity of

x\mapsto x\log(C/x)
near zero bounds the preceding display by

Ch^{(p-1)/2}\{\log(h^{-1})\}^{2}
. Letting

\varepsilon\downarrow 0
proves the assertion.
∎

C.4
Proofs of the main inference statements

Proof of Theorem
3
.

By Lemma
5
,

\hat{\theta}_{n}^{DB,cf}(a_{0})-\theta_{0}(a_{0})=P_{n}\phi_{\infty,a_{0}}+\sum_{j=1}^{6}R_{n,j}(a_{0}).

Lemma
15
gives
R_{n,1}(a_{0})=o(h_{n}^{2})
, and Lemmas
22
,
24
,
17
,
19
, and
16
give
R_{n,j}(a_{0})=o_{p}\{(nh_{n})^{-1/2}\}
for
j=2,\ldots,6
. This proves the displayed first-order expansion. Lemmas
7
and
8
yield the stated normal limit for
(nh_{n})^{1/2}P_{n}\phi_{\infty,a_{0}}
. If
nh_{n}^{5}=O(1)
, then
(nh_{n})^{1/2}R_{n,1}(a_{0})=o(1)
, and the stated limit for the estimator follows by Slutsky’s theorem.
∎

Proof of Theorem
4
.

Lemma
5
gives the expansion componentwise at

a_{1},\ldots,a_{m}
. The pointwise remainder bounds in the proof of
Theorem
3
apply componentwise. The
deterministic smoothing term is negligible under
nh_{n}^{5}=O(1)
.
Lemmas
9

and
8
give the finite-dimensional
Gaussian limit with diagonal covariance matrix.
∎

Proof of Theorem
5
.

By Lemma
5
, uniformly over
a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}
,

\hat{\theta}_{n}^{DB,cf}(a_{0})-\theta_{0}(a_{0})-P_{n}\phi_{\infty,h_{n},b_{n},a_{0}}=\sum_{j=1}^{6}R_{n,j}(a_{0}).

Assumptions
G1
and
G5
and Lemma
15
give

\displaystyle\sqrt{nh_{n}\log n}\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}|R_{n,1}(a_{0})|

\displaystyle=O\!\left(\sqrt{nh_{n}^{5}}\,h_{n}^{\alpha}\sqrt{\log n}\right)

\displaystyle=o(1).

Lemmas
22
,
24
,
17
,
19
, and
16
give

\max_{2\leq j\leq 6}\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}|R_{n,j}(a_{0})|=o_{p}\{(nh_{n}\log n)^{-1/2}\}.

The uniform expansion follows.

The corollary to Lemma
13
gives the Gaussian approximation for

\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\frac{(nh_{n})^{1/2}P_{n}\phi_{\infty,h_{n},b_{n},a_{0}}}{\sigma_{\infty,h_{n},b_{n}}(a_{0})}\right|.

By Lemma
10
and the uniform expansion, the difference between this random variable and the corresponding supremum with
P_{n}\phi_{\infty,h_{n},b_{n},a_{0}}
replaced by
\hat{\theta}_{n}^{DB,cf}(a_{0})-\theta_{0}(a_{0})
is
o_{p}\{(\log n)^{-1/2}\}
. Assumption
G1
implies
\log(h_{n}^{-1})=O(\log n)
. The anti-concentration bound used in Lemma
13
therefore gives the stated Kolmogorov-distance convergence. Lemma
14
and Assumption
G6
give the finite-mesh assertion.
∎

Appendix D
Remainder control

D.1
The smoothing and local-polynomial matrix remainders

We first control the deterministic smoothing remainder

R_{n,1}(a_{0})=\theta_{0,h_{n},b_{n}}(a_{0})-\theta_{0}(a_{0}).

Lemma 15
.

If
D1
,
D2
, and

E1
(a)–(b) hold, then

R_{n,1}(a_{0})=o(h_{n}^{2})
.
If Assumption
G5
(i)
–
(ii)
also holds, then

\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}|R_{n,1}(a_{0})|=O(h_{n}^{2+\alpha}+h_{n}^{2}b_{n}^{\alpha}).

If Assumption
G1
also holds, then

\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}|R_{n,1}(a_{0})|=o\{(nh_{n}\log n)^{-1/2}\}.

Proof.

Write
h=h_{n}
,
b=b_{n}
, and

\Gamma_{0}=\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}
. Direct substitution of the definitions of

\Gamma_{0}
,
D_{0,h,a_{0},1}
,
D_{0,b,a_{0},2}
, and

c_{0,h,a_{0},2}
gives the quadratic reproduction identities

P_{0}\{\Gamma_{0}(A)\}=1,\qquad P_{0}\{\Gamma_{0}(A)(A-a_{0})\}=0,\qquad P_{0}\{\Gamma_{0}(A)(A-a_{0})^{2}\}=0.

Indeed, the local-linear component reproduces the constant and linear terms
and maps the quadratic term to
h^{2}c_{0,h,a_{0},2}
, whereas the
local-quadratic bias-correction component maps the quadratic term to the same
quantity and maps the constant and linear terms to zero.

For every
a
in a sufficiently small neighborhood of
a_{0}
, the
integral form of Taylor’s expansion gives

\displaystyle\theta_{0}(a)={}

\displaystyle\theta_{0}(a_{0})+\theta_{0}^{\prime}(a_{0})(a-a_{0})+\frac{1}{2}\theta_{0}^{\prime\prime}(a_{0})(a-a_{0})^{2}

\displaystyle+(a-a_{0})^{2}\int_{0}^{1}(1-t)\left\{\theta_{0}^{\prime\prime}\bigl(a_{0}+t(a-a_{0})\bigr)-\theta_{0}^{\prime\prime}(a_{0})\right\}\,dt.

The reproduction identities therefore imply

\displaystyle R_{n,1}(a_{0})=\int

\displaystyle\Gamma_{0}(a)(a-a_{0})^{2}\int_{0}^{1}(1-t)\left\{\theta_{0}^{\prime\prime}\bigl(a_{0}+t(a-a_{0})\bigr)-\theta_{0}^{\prime\prime}(a_{0})\right\}\,dt\,dF_{0}(a).

By Lemma
6
, positivity of
f_{A}(a_{0})
, and

h/b=O(1)
,

\|D_{0,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}\|_{\infty}+\|D_{0,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}\|_{\infty}+|c_{0,h,a_{0},2}|\leq C

for all sufficiently large
n
. Since
K
is bounded and supported on

[-1,1]
,

|\Gamma_{0}(a)|\leq Ch^{-1}I(|a-a_{0}|\leq h)+Ch^{2}b^{-3}I(|a-a_{0}|\leq b).

Since
f_{A}
is bounded near
a_{0}
, it follows that

\displaystyle|R_{n,1}(a_{0})|\leq{}

\displaystyle Ch^{2}\sup_{|u-a_{0}|\leq h}|\theta_{0}^{\prime\prime}(u)-\theta_{0}^{\prime\prime}(a_{0})|+Ch^{2}\sup_{|u-a_{0}|\leq b}|\theta_{0}^{\prime\prime}(u)-\theta_{0}^{\prime\prime}(a_{0})|.

Because
h\to 0
,
b\to 0
, and
\theta_{0}^{\prime\prime}
is continuous at
a_{0}
,
the right-hand side is
o(h^{2})
.

For the uniform result, the matrix bounds above hold uniformly over

a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}
. Hölder continuity gives

\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}\\
|u-a_{0}|\leq h\end{subarray}}|\theta_{0}^{\prime\prime}(u)-\theta_{0}^{\prime\prime}(a_{0})|\leq Ch^{\alpha},\qquad\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}\\
|u-a_{0}|\leq b\end{subarray}}|\theta_{0}^{\prime\prime}(u)-\theta_{0}^{\prime\prime}(a_{0})|\leq Cb^{\alpha}.

Substitution into the preceding bound yields

\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}|R_{n,1}(a_{0})|\leq C\{h^{2+\alpha}+h^{2}b^{\alpha}\}.

Under Assumption
G1
,
h/b\to\tau\in(0,\infty)
, so
the last display is
O(h^{2+\alpha})
. Moreover,

\sqrt{nh\log n}\,h^{2+\alpha}=\sqrt{nh^{5}}\,h^{\alpha}\sqrt{\log n}\longrightarrow 0,

because
nh^{5}=O(1)
implies
h=O(n^{-1/5})
. This proves the final
assertion.
∎

We next control the local-polynomial matrix remainder
R_{n,6}
. Throughout
the proof,
\|\cdot\|_{\infty}
denotes the elementwise maximum norm.

Lemma 16
.

If
D1
,
D2
, and

E1
(a)–(b) hold, then

R_{n,6}(a_{0})=O_{p}\{(nh_{n})^{-1}\}.

If Assumption
G5
(i)
–
(ii)
also holds and

nh_{n}/\log(h_{n}^{-1})\to\infty
, then

\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}|R_{n,6}(a_{0})|=O_{p}\!\left\{\frac{\log(h_{n}^{-1})}{nh_{n}}\right\}.

Proof.

Write
h=h_{n}
and
b=b_{n}
. We first establish the pointwise empirical
matrix rates. Each entry of

D_{n,h,a_{0},1}-D_{0,h,a_{0},1}

is the empirical mean of a centered function of the form

\left(\frac{A-a_{0}}{h}\right)^{j}K_{h,a_{0}}(A),\qquad j\in\{0,1,2\}.

Because
K
is bounded and compactly supported and
f_{A}
is bounded near

a_{0}
,

P_{0}\left[\left\{\left(\frac{A-a_{0}}{h}\right)^{j}K_{h,a_{0}}(A)\right\}^{2}\right]\leq Ch^{-1}.

Consequently, Chebyshev’s inequality yields

\|D_{n,h,a_{0},1}-D_{0,h,a_{0},1}\|_{\infty}=O_{p}\{(nh)^{-1/2}\}.

The same argument gives

\|(P_{n}-P_{0})\{\widetilde{w}_{h,a_{0},1}(A)K_{h,a_{0}}(A)\}\|_{\infty}=O_{p}\{(nh)^{-1/2}\}

and

\|D_{n,b,a_{0},2}-D_{0,b,a_{0},2}\|_{\infty}=O_{p}\{(nb)^{-1/2}\}.

Lemma
6
implies that the population moment matrices are
nonsingular with bounded inverses for all sufficiently large
n
. Since
the empirical matrices converge to their population counterparts, their
inverses are
O_{p}(1)
. The identity

D_{n}^{-1}-D_{0}^{-1}=D_{n}^{-1}(D_{0}-D_{n})D_{0}^{-1}

therefore gives

\|D_{n,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}-D_{0,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}\|_{\infty}=O_{p}\{(nh)^{-1/2}\},

and

\|D_{n,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}-D_{0,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}\|_{\infty}=O_{p}\{(nb)^{-1/2}\}.

Moreover,

\displaystyle c_{n,h,a_{0},2}-c_{0,h,a_{0},2}={}

\displaystyle e_{1}^{\top}(D_{n,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}-D_{0,h,a_{0},1}^{-1})P_{n}\{\widetilde{w}_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}\}

\displaystyle+e_{1}^{\top}D_{0,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}(P_{n}-P_{0})\{\widetilde{w}_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}\}.

Since

P_{n}\{\widetilde{w}_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}\}=O_{p}(1)
, the preceding rates imply

|c_{n,h,a_{0},2}-c_{0,h,a_{0},2}|=O_{p}\{(nh)^{-1/2}\}.

Continuity of
\theta_{0}
, boundedness of
f_{A}
, and compact support of

K
imply

\displaystyle\left\|P_{0}\{w_{h,a_{0},1}(A)K_{h,a_{0}}(A)\theta_{0}(A)\}\right\|_{\infty}+\left\|P_{0}\{w_{b,a_{0},2}(A)K_{b,a_{0}}(A)\theta_{0}(A)\}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle+\left\|P_{0}\{\widetilde{w}_{h,a_{0},1}(A)K_{h,a_{0}}(A)\}\right\|_{\infty}\leq C.

Also,
h/b=O(1)
. Hence, from the definition of
R_{n,6}
,

\displaystyle|R_{n,6}(a_{0})|\lesssim{}

\displaystyle\|D_{n,h,a_{0},1}-D_{0,h,a_{0},1}\|_{\infty}\|D_{n,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}-D_{0,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle+\|D_{n,b,a_{0},2}-D_{0,b,a_{0},2}\|_{\infty}\|D_{n,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}-D_{0,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle+\|D_{n,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}-D_{0,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}\|_{\infty}\|(P_{n}-P_{0})\{\widetilde{w}_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}\}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle+\|D_{n,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}-D_{0,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}\|_{\infty}\|D_{n,h,a_{0},1}-D_{0,h,a_{0},1}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle+|c_{n,h,a_{0},2}-c_{0,h,a_{0},2}|\|D_{n,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}-D_{0,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}\|_{\infty}.

The first, third, and fourth terms are
O_{p}\{(nh)^{-1}\}
, the second is

O_{p}\{(nb)^{-1}\}
, and the fifth is

O_{p}\{n^{-1}(hb)^{-1/2}\}
. Since
h/b=O(1)
,

(nb)^{-1}+n^{-1}(hb)^{-1/2}=O\{(nh)^{-1}\}.

This proves the pointwise assertion.

For the uniform result, Assumption
D1
implies that the
translated and rescaled kernel classes appearing above are VC–type
(
Takatsu and Westling, 2025
)
. Following Lemma 20 in
Takatsu and Westling (2025)
, a
standard VC–type maximal inequality, together with

nh/\log(h^{-1})\to\infty
, yields

\displaystyle\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\|D_{n,h,a_{0},1}-D_{0,h,a_{0},1}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=O_{p}\!\left[\left\{\frac{\log(h^{-1})}{nh}\right\}^{1/2}\right],

\displaystyle\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\|(P_{n}-P_{0})\{\widetilde{w}_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}\}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=O_{p}\!\left[\left\{\frac{\log(h^{-1})}{nh}\right\}^{1/2}\right].

Uniform nonsingularity of the population matrices and the inverse identity
then give

\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\|D_{n,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}-D_{0,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}\|_{\infty}=O_{p}\!\left[\left\{\frac{\log(h^{-1})}{nh}\right\}^{1/2}\right],

and the preceding expansion of
c_{n,h,a_{0},2}-c_{0,h,a_{0},2}
gives the
same rate uniformly.

The corresponding
b
-bandwidth quantities have rate

O_{p}\!\left[\left\{\frac{\log(b^{-1})}{nb}\right\}^{1/2}\right].

Since
h/b=O(1)
,

\frac{\log(b^{-1})}{nb}=O\!\left\{\frac{\log(h^{-1})}{nh}\right\}.

Substituting these uniform rates into the preceding bound for
R_{n,6}

shows that each summand is

O_{p}\!\left\{\frac{\log(h^{-1})}{nh}\right\}

uniformly over
a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}
, which proves the result.
∎

D.2
Control of the proximal drift and product-empirical remainders

D.2.1
Control of the proximal doubly robust remainder

Lemma 17
.

Under Assumptions
A
,
B
, and
D
–
F
,

R_{n,4}(a_{0})=o_{p}\{(nh_{n})^{-1/2}\}.

Under Assumptions
A
,
B
,
D
, and
G
,

\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}|R_{n,4}(a_{0})|=o_{p}\{(nh_{n}\log n)^{-1/2}\}.

Proof.

Write
h=h_{n}
,
b=b_{n}
, and
\tau_{n}=h/b
. We first establish a
bound on the
L_{1}(F_{0})
-norm of the estimated equivalent kernel. By
the empirical moment-matrix bounds used in the analysis of

R_{n,6}
,

\displaystyle\|D_{n,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}\|_{\infty}+\|D_{n,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}\|_{\infty}+|c_{n,h,a_{0},2}|=O_{p}(1).

Also, boundedness and compact support of
K
, together with local
boundedness of
f_{A}
, imply

\displaystyle\int\|w_{h,a_{0},1}(a)\||K_{h,a_{0}}(a)|\,dF_{0}(a)

\displaystyle=O(1),

\displaystyle\int\|w_{b,a_{0},2}(a)\||K_{b,a_{0}}(a)|\,dF_{0}(a)

\displaystyle=O(1).

Consequently,

\int|\Gamma_{n,h,b,a_{0}}(a)|\,dF_{0}(a)=O_{p}(1),

(29)

since
\tau_{n}=O(1)
. Under the uniform matrix bounds, the same
argument gives

\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\int|\Gamma_{n,h,b,a_{0}}(a)|\,dF_{0}(a)=O_{p}(1).

(30)

We next record the conditional proximal product-bias identity. Fix a fold

k
and condition on
\mathscr{T}_{k}
. The nuisance estimators are then
fixed measurable functions, while an independent generic observation

O
has law
P_{0}
. For
F_{0}
-almost every
a
,
the outcome bridge equation gives

\displaystyle E_{0}\Big[\hat{q}_{n}^{(-k)}(Z,a,X)\{Y-h_{0}(W,a,X)\}\mid A=a,\mathscr{T}_{k}\Big]=0.

Moreover, the treatment bridge equation and the density-ratio identity give

\displaystyle E_{0}\Big[q_{0}(Z,a,X)\{h_{0}(W,a,X)-\hat{h}_{n}^{(-k)}(W,a,X)\}\mid A=a,\mathscr{T}_{k}\Big]

\displaystyle=E_{0}\Big[h_{0}(W,a,X)-\hat{h}_{n}^{(-k)}(W,a,X)\mid\mathscr{T}_{k}\Big].

Subtracting and adding the true bridges therefore yields

\displaystyle E_{0}\left[\hat{\xi}_{n,1}^{(-k)}(O)+S_{\hat{h}_{n}^{(-k)}}(A)\mid A=a,\mathscr{T}_{k}\right]-\theta_{0}(a)

\displaystyle\quad=E_{0}\Big[\{\hat{q}_{n}^{(-k)}(Z,a,X)-q_{0}(Z,a,X)\}\{h_{0}(W,a,X)-\hat{h}_{n}^{(-k)}(W,a,X)\}\mid A=a,\mathscr{T}_{k}\Big].

(31)

This argument uses only the two bridge equations. In particular,

\hat{q}_{n}^{(-k)}
and
\hat{h}_{n}^{(-k)}
need not themselves solve
either bridge equation.

For brevity, denote the right-hand side of
(
31
) by

\Delta_{n,k}(a)
. From the definition of
R_{n,4}
,

\displaystyle|R_{n,4}(a_{0})|\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K}\frac{n_{k}}{n}\int|\Gamma_{n,h,b,a_{0}}(a)||\Delta_{n,k}(a)|\,dF_{0}(a).

For all sufficiently large
n
, the support of

\Gamma_{n,h,b,a_{0}}
is contained in

B_{\delta_{1}}(a_{0})
. Hence

|R_{n,4}(a_{0})|\leq\left\{\int|\Gamma_{n,h,b,a_{0}}(a)|\,dF_{0}(a)\right\}\max_{1\leq k\leq K}\sup_{a\in B_{\delta_{1}}(a_{0})}|\Delta_{n,k}(a)|.

(32)

It remains to control the conditional drift. Since

\mathcal{S}_{1},\mathcal{S}_{2},\mathcal{S}_{3}
partition the relevant
conditional support,

\displaystyle\Delta_{n,k}(a)=\sum_{j=1}^{3}E_{0}\Big[

\displaystyle I_{\mathcal{S}_{j}}(a,Z,W,X)\{\hat{q}_{n}^{(-k)}(Z,a,X)-q_{0}(Z,a,X)\}

\displaystyle\times\{h_{0}(W,a,X)-\hat{h}_{n}^{(-k)}(W,a,X)\}\mid A=a,\mathscr{T}_{k}\Big].

The Cauchy–Schwarz inequality gives

\displaystyle\sup_{a\in\mathcal{I}}|\Delta_{n,k}(a)|\leq\sum_{j=1}^{3}

\displaystyle d_{q,k}(\hat{q}_{n}^{(-k)},q_{0};\mathcal{I},\mathcal{S}_{j})d_{h,k}(\hat{h}_{n}^{(-k)},h_{0};\mathcal{I},\mathcal{S}_{j}).

On
\mathcal{S}_{1}
,
q_{\infty}=q_{0}
, and therefore

d_{q,k}(\hat{q}_{n}^{(-k)},q_{0};\mathcal{I},\mathcal{S}_{1})=d_{q,k}(\hat{q}_{n}^{(-k)},q_{\infty};\mathcal{I},\mathcal{S}_{1}).

Furthermore,

\displaystyle d_{h,k}(\hat{h}_{n}^{(-k)},h_{0};\mathcal{I},\mathcal{S}_{1})\leq d_{h,k}(\hat{h}_{n}^{(-k)},h_{\infty};\mathcal{I},\mathcal{S}_{1})+d_{h,k}(h_{\infty},h_{0};\mathcal{I},\mathcal{S}_{1})=O_{p}(1).

Thus, the
\mathcal{S}_{1}
contribution is

o_{p}\{(nh)^{-1/2}\}
. The same argument with the roles of the
bridges reversed shows that the
\mathcal{S}_{2}
contribution is

o_{p}\{(nh)^{-1/2}\}
. On
\mathcal{S}_{3}
, both limiting
